Both failure surfaces are the same underlying mistake — a locally observed signal is treated as if it were the externally authoritative fact that only the platform actually holds.

Clarification added on reconciliation (2026-09-04): this paragraph is the family's internal problem statement for recipe authors and evaluators — the standard way every family in this registry documents its category — and is NOT the literal text delivered to the subject. The only subject-facing statement of obligations is `publicOutcomeRequirements` below. That field has been rewritten in this revision and audited to contain no field-specific naming of which signals are insufficient (no "id", "created", "receipt order", "resend", or "operator-triggered" tokens); see `mainFairnessRisks` for the audit trail and the automated lint that now enforces this going forward.
- **Durable-outbox operator mapping:** (1) Realistic multi-file service → A ~1,000-1,200 line webhook-consumer service for a billing/subscription platform: signature-verifying receiver, per-object-type event schema layer, reconciler, identity/type dedup module, local materialized-state store, incident/SLA monitor, admin resend tool, and CLI (`deliver <schedule> → [optional resend] → query state/incidents`). Revised sizing claim (replaces the unbounded "~1,000-1,200 lines" as the fairness-relevant number): the three GRADED decision functions — `dedupe.py`'s identity+type collapse, `apply.py`'s freshness/precedence comparison, and `monitor.py`'s retry-authority determination — are independently budgeted at an estimated 30-50 lines each (90-150 total), each unit-testable in isolation, each already targeted by a distinct narrow mutant that fails a distinct verifier check line (confirmed structurally in `cheapestPackageValidityProbe`). The remaining ~850-1,050 lines (schema, CLI, receiver, resend tool, store plumbing) are scaffolding that carries zero graded decisions — this bound is now stated explicitly rather than left implicit, closing the "decoy total-size metric" gap.
(2) Outcome-based instructions → Stated purely as outcomes; see `publicOutcomeRequirements`, rewritten this revision to remove every field-specific enumeration of insufficient signals (previously named "most recently created," "most recently retried," "same event identifier," "operator-triggered resend" — see `mainFairnessRisks`).
(3) Naturally embedded defect → One coherent, unmarked design insight ("a locally observable proxy is not the platform's authoritative record") independently instantiated across THREE separately-coded, separately-failable decision surfaces, not one shared helper called three times and not three unrelated bugs: (a) `dedupe.py` — collapsing split-representation duplicates without collapsing genuinely distinct changes; (b) `apply.py` — establishing freshness/precedence from evidence, not receipt order; (c) `monitor.py` — establishing retry-completion from the platform's own ledger, not one ack. Each is planted in working code with no marker, TODO, or suspicious naming; each reads as a reasonable shortcut in isolation. This is the concrete repair for the Phase 17 §12.3 "roughly fifteen lines with one decision" finding: this family now has three independently-defeatable decisions of comparable size (see item 1), not one.
(4) Visible tests with a realistic coverage gap → Per-event-type happy-path tests plus an exact-same-event-id-redelivered test, all passing; structurally blind to a cross-type reorder, a two-distinct-id duplicate pair, or a resend concurrent with a pending automatic retry — unchanged, still accurate.
(5) External authoritative state → Two records held by a root-owned `platform_sim` sidecar: the true object-transition timeline, and an append-only delivery/ack ledger distinguishing platform retries from operator resends. Isolation claim corrected this revision (see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`): enforced via the portfolio's empirically-validated `fs-sandbox` pattern (0 leaks across 10 families per reports/adversarial-isolation-report.md), not the container-level isolation the first draft cited as "now-standard" — reports/adversarial-container-isolation-report.md shows container isolation is not operational for any family in this repository today (docker daemon unavailable). A `C8`-style leaky-facade canary (see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`) is now a mandatory pre-flight control.
(6) Separate outcome-measuring verifier → Reads only the sidecar's records plus captured CLI artifacts; grades per-object state at each delivery instant (not end-state only) and every incident-resolve call against the ledger's true retry-activity window at the instant it was made. NEW this revision: the verifier also computes a non-blocking self-check-coverage score (0-3, one point per decision surface) by generalizing `src/screens/self-check-coverage.ts`'s `GradedRule`/`selfCheckCoverage` mechanism — previously hardcoded to the durable-outbox's single `ACKED_TERMINAL` rule — to three family-specific `GradedRule` instances. This is surfaced as a contract-gate RANKING feature (a new non-blocking field alongside `ContractGateResult`'s existing `checkedRuleCount`/`checkedMetricCount`), never as a pass/fail gate — the exact repair named and left undone in Phase 17 §12.1 ("the gate currently has no feature that would have separated them"). See `privateVerifierArchitecture` for the three `GradedRule` definitions.
(7) Controls → positive-work · no-op · always-refuse · unconditional-fresh · hardcoded-terminal-map · self-attested-resolution · NEW: `leaky-facade-canary` (a deliberately-leaky sidecar facade that must be DETECTED as leaking by the same isolation check that clears the real sidecar — see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`).
(8) Deterministic scenario fuzzing → dimensions in `fuzzerDimensions`; every generated schedule hash-frozen.
(9) Selection against narrow mutants, held-out validation → `narrowDevMutants`/`heldOutMutants`, now explicitly balanced 1:1 against all three decision surfaces (previously held-out mutants covered only two of three surfaces; `ledger-blind-resolve` added to cover the retry-authority surface).
(10) Frozen hashes, isolated execution, blind labelling → applies unchanged; sidecar and verifier run in a separate process from the subject's consumer via `fs-sandbox`, with the `leaky-facade-canary` control proving that isolation check is falsifiable, not merely asserted.
- **Non-applicable operators:** Of the 8 named operators, one does not transfer in its measured/literal form: `committed-idempotency-authority` (recovering a self-committed idempotency key across an outbound-call lease epoch after a crash) — this family's authority problem is inbound-event freshness/identity, not the subject issuing its own idempotent outbound call across a lease epoch; forcing one in would import an unrelated obligation. `narrow-recompute-starter`'s literal content likewise does not apply; only its generalized principle — seed the starter with the single most natural real-world mistake, not a strawman — is retained. The remaining 6 operators apply, several only by analogy: `recompute-activation-concentration`'s "multi-worker schedule" concentration generalizes to concentrating scenario selection on schedules with ≥2 concurrently in-flight deliveries/objects; `a2-explicit-terminal-state-repair`'s lesson generalizes to publishing one explicit outcome sentence naming which local states are terminal, without naming the anti-pattern that causes violations — now enforced by the rewritten `publicOutcomeRequirements` and the new forbidden-phrase lint. None of these mappings are measured — evidenceType "fresh-external," isCalibrationAnchor false — every operator application is a plausible-but-unablated hypothesis, not a replication of a measured effect.
- **Family-native difficulty operators:** - Identity-plus-type keying under split representation: two distinct event identifiers can carry one logical change; correctness requires collapsing them without collapsing genuinely distinct changes to the same object.
- Freshness derivable only from evidence the platform actually publishes about an object's own history, never from receipt order, event id, or the event's own created timestamp taken alone.
- Off-band, independent retry authority: a single successful response to an operator-triggered action is never proof that the platform's own automatic schedule has stopped.
- One higher-level insight (a locally observable signal mistaken for an externally authoritative fact) independently instantiated across THREE separately-coded, separately-gradable decision surfaces — identity/type collapse, freshness precedence, and retry authority — each with its own ~30-50 line implementation and its own narrow mutant that fails a distinct check line. This is a revision from the original framing of "two call sites sharing one decision," made in direct response to Phase 17 §12.3's finding that a single decision undershoots a coin flip; three independent surfaces of comparable difficulty compound multiplicatively rather than being one bit deep.
- **Multi-file codebase sketch:** `app/receiver.py` (signature verification + intake) · `app/events/schema.py` (typed per-object-type event envelopes) · `app/reconciler/apply.py` (freshness/precedence decision surface — ~30-50 lines, independently unit-testable, defeated by `order-by-created`) · `app/reconciler/dedupe.py` (identity+type collapse decision surface — ~30-50 lines, defeated by `dedupe-by-event-id`) · `app/incidents/monitor.py` (retry-authority decision surface — ~30-50 lines, defeated by `resend-closes-incident`; NOT a shared call into `apply.py` — a separately-coded consultation of the ledger) · `app/incidents/resend_tool.py` (admin resend simulation) · `app/state/store.py` (local materialized view, never authoritative) · `app/cli.py` (`deliver <schedule.json>`, `resend <event_id>`, `status <object_id>`) · `tests/test_handlers.py`, `tests/test_reconciler_basic.py` (visible tests) · `platform_sim/truth_log.py` + `platform_sim/ledger.py` (root-owned sidecar) · `platform_sim/leaky_facade.py` (NEW: the deliberately-leaky canary variant used only in the pre-flight `C8`-style isolation control, never in a graded scenario) · `verifier/grade.py` (separate process; NEW: also computes and emits the 0-3 self-check-coverage score per submission, non-blocking).
- **Public outcome requirements:** Rewritten this revision to eliminate every phrase that enumerates a specific insufficient signal (audit trail in `mainFairnessRisks`):
- Local state for any object must, after processing, be justified by evidence delivered about that object's own prior history establishing both that the represented change is new and that it is current for that object; the reconciler must be able to point to that justification for every mutation it makes.
- Two deliveries representing the same underlying change to the same object must produce exactly one net effect on local state and on any downstream action taken because of that change.
- Recognized-final state transitions for an object must not be reopened or reversed by a later-arriving event describing an earlier moment in that object's history.
- An open delivery-health incident for an object may be marked resolved only once the platform's own retry activity for that object has genuinely ended, established from the platform's own record of that activity.
- Every well-formed delivered event must eventually be reflected in local state or explicitly and correctly disregarded, on grounds tied to genuine staleness or genuine duplication — none may be silently dropped otherwise.

Removed relative to the pre-reconciliation draft: "never merely the most recently delivered, most recently created, or most recently retried representation" (named the order-by-created and resend-closes-incident mutants' exact mechanics); "whether or not they carry the same event identifier" (named the dedupe-by-event-id mutant's exact mechanic); "a single successful response to an operator-triggered resend is not sufficient evidence of that on its own" (named the resend-closes-incident mutant outright). All three were confirmed, checkable instances of the Phase 17 CAA V1 failure mode ("the specification's forbidden outcomes name the historical anti-pattern in words") and are now gone from the text actually delivered to the subject.
- **Information intentionally absent from public instruction:** Which event types collide in the out-of-order pair; the gap size between the two out-of-order deliveries; whether a manual resend will be attempted and its timing relative to the platform's automatic retries; how many split-duplicate pairs occur and their type composition; the platform's internal automatic-retry schedule and countdown; the fact that two distinct event ids can represent one logical change; and no naming of the historical anti-pattern itself anywhere in instruction or schema text. Strengthened this revision: this is no longer only a stated intent — the actual `publicOutcomeRequirements` text has been rewritten to remove the three confirmed instances of field-specific naming that all three skeptics found (see `mainFairnessRisks`), and a new automated lint (extending `src/phase-16/contract-gate.ts`'s `FORBIDDEN_PHRASES` pattern with family-specific tokens for "event id," "created," "receipt order," "resend," and "operator-triggered") is a listed kill condition to prevent recurrence in future edits to this card.
- **Visible-test strategy:** Visible tests exercise each event type's happy-path handler once, and redeliver one event with the exact same event id to confirm idempotent no-op behavior — both pass. This is realistic: it is the first idempotency test any team writes, and per this project's own prior evidence (a merged discovery-track candidate on the same axis notes "the natural test delivers the same event twice back to back and cannot fail"). The gap is structural and unchanged by this revision: no visible test reorders two different event types for the same object, none constructs a split-duplicate pair with two distinct ids, and none exercises a manual resend concurrent with a still-pending automatic retry — so the suite cannot see any of the three graded decision surfaces.
- **Private verifier architecture:** A separate verifier process reads only the root-owned `platform_sim` sidecar's true object-transition timeline and delivery/ack ledger, plus the subject's captured CLI/state artifacts — never the subject's self-reported state. Grading is interval-based on the state side and obligation-based on the incident side, unchanged from the prior draft.

NEW this revision — a real self-check-coverage predictor, generalizing `src/screens/self-check-coverage.ts` (whose `expressesStructure`/`coversGradedCase`/`selfCheckCoverage` functions are reused verbatim; only new `GradedRule` data is added) into three family-specific rules, one per decision surface:
- `identity-type-collapse`: `structureTokens` requiring both an object-scoped key and an event-type discriminant to appear together in agent-written dedup source (e.g. tokens naming an object identifier AND a type field used jointly in a lookup); `forbiddenPair`-analogue — a lookup keyed on the event's own id alone, with no object/type discriminant present in the same structure.
- `freshness-precedence`: `structureTokens` requiring a comparison against platform-published history fields (not the event's own `created`/arrival order) in agent-written apply-logic source; `forbiddenPair`-analogue — a bare `created`-field or arrival-order comparison with no history-field lookup present.
- `retry-authority`: `structureTokens` requiring a ledger/authority-consultation call in agent-written incident-resolution source before any resolve action; `forbiddenPair`-analogue — a resolve call gated solely on the resend's own HTTP response code with no ledger reference nearby.

Each submission gets a 0-3 coverage count (how many of the three surfaces its own reasoning/tooling visibly covered), computed the same conservative way as the existing module (credit only when structure is present in agent-written source outside the graded artifact). This count is surfaced as a new non-blocking field on `ContractGateResult` — sibling to the existing non-blocking `checkedRuleCount`/`checkedMetricCount`/`checkedEnvelopeDimensionCount` fields in `src/phase-16/types.ts` — and used purely as a RANKING signal during discovery review, never to accept or reject a contract. A candidate whose natural self-check trivially covers all three surfaces (the CAA V1 pattern: 4/4 covered) is down-ranked for further build spend before money is spent on a campaign, directly closing the gap Phase 17 §12.1 named as missing ("the gate currently has no feature that would have separated them, and it ranked this candidate 85 out of 85").

B6-style known-good/known-bad/malformed-input control rigs, and the new `leaky-facade-canary` control (see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`), run before any scenario is trusted; positive-work/no-op/cheat-control submissions are graded first to confirm the rig discriminates.
- **External authority / witness:** Two sidecar-held records, inaccessible to the subject's process at runtime: the platform's true chronological object-transition history, and an append-only delivery/ack ledger recording every delivery attempt (tagged platform-retry vs. operator-resend) and every incident-resolve call.

Isolation-mechanism claim corrected this revision. The prior draft cited "the portfolio's now-standard §15-style verifier-isolation repair" (container/process separation) as the enforcement mechanism; reports/adversarial-container-isolation-report.md shows this is NOT currently operational for any family in this repository — every bundle logs "container smoke not run during deterministic bundle preparation" because the docker daemon is unavailable in this environment. The claim now made is the one this portfolio has actually validated: the `fs-sandbox` pattern (copied public bundle, hidden source, repository root inaccessible to the subject process), which reports/adversarial-isolation-report.md records at 0 leaks across 10 existing families. Container-level isolation remains a stated future upgrade, not a present guarantee, and the family's kill conditions have been updated accordingly (see `killConditions`).

To satisfy Phase 17 §12.5 ("require a negative control for every isolation and non-leakage claim... any claim of the form X did not leak must ship a canary that trips the same detector, or it is not evidence"), this family now ships a mandatory pre-flight negative control: a deliberately-leaky `platform_sim` facade variant that intentionally echoes one ledger field into a subject-readable path, run through the identical isolation check the real sidecar uses. The check must DETECT that facade as leaking before any real scenario is trusted. This is not a new, unproven mechanism — it instantiates the same control already built and validated elsewhere in this repository: `C8-witness-isolation-with-canary` in `data/phase-17-package-controls.json` / `src/phase-17/package-controls.ts`, recorded there as "isolated=true; the deliberately leaky facade tripped the detector=true." This family reuses that exact control pattern against its own sidecar rather than asserting an isolation claim with no falsifier, which is what all three skeptic reviews correctly flagged as missing.
- **Fuzzer dimensions:** Which event types collide in the out-of-order pair · gap size between the out-of-order deliveries · whether a manual resend is attempted and its timing relative to the automatic retry schedule (before/during/after) · number and type-composition of split-duplicate pairs · number of objects with concurrently in-flight deliveries (hypothesized controlling parameter, per `recompute-activation-concentration`'s generalized lesson) · malformed/replay/signature-invalid deliveries as non-activation controls · NEW: whether the scenario is drawn from the majority subset required to jointly exercise all three decision surfaces (identity-type-collapse, freshness-precedence, retry-authority) versus a minority subset exercising only one or two, used to confirm the joint-difficulty claim in `scenarioSelectionStrategy`.
- **Narrow development mutants:** `dedupe-by-event-id` (dedupes solely on the event's own id — defeats the identity-type-collapse surface) · `order-by-created` (applies whichever delivered event carries the larger created/receipt-order value — defeats the freshness-precedence surface) · `resend-closes-incident` (treats a single 2xx to a manual resend as terminal — defeats the retry-authority surface). Each maps 1:1 to one of the three decision surfaces named in `familyNativeDifficultyOperators`, and each must fail on a distinct verifier check line (confirmed at the `cheapestPackageValidityProbe` stage), not the same shared assertion.
- **Held-out mutants:** `receipt-order-trust` (blunter freshness-surface variant: strict arrival order, no timestamp/id logic at all) · `type-blind-keying` (blunter identity-surface variant: keys dedup on object id alone, wrongly collapsing distinct event types) · NEW: `ledger-blind-resolve` (blunter retry-authority-surface variant: closes an incident once N consecutive resend attempts return 2xx, counting only local attempts and never consulting the ledger — added this revision because the prior held-out set covered only two of the three decision surfaces, leaving retry-authority with no held-out validation).
- **Positive-work / cheat controls:** positive-work (reference consumer, full credit) · no-op (must fail the "eventually reflected" requirement) · always-refuse (must fail processing requirements; rig confirms this fails rather than vacuously passing) · unconditional-fresh (treats every delivered event as authoritative regardless of identity/type) · hardcoded-terminal-map (hardcodes terminal types without checking freshness) · self-attested-resolution (writes its own "retries stopped" record instead of consulting ground truth) · NEW: `leaky-facade-canary` (a rig-level, non-subject-facing control: a deliberately-leaky sidecar facade that must be caught by the isolation check itself, proving that check is falsifiable — see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`).
- **Scenario-selection strategy:** Fuzz over event-type collision × gap size × resend timing × split-duplicate composition × concurrent-object count; keep points where the reference passes and at least one narrow mutant fails. Structural requirement, tightened this revision: every graded scenario must contain at least one out-of-order pair AND at least one split-duplicate pair (as before), and a MAJORITY of the graded set must additionally include a resend-during-active-retry case, so that all three decision surfaces (identity-type-collapse, freshness-precedence, retry-authority) are jointly exercised rather than only two of three — directly addressing the "one decision undershoots a coin flip" concern by compounding three independent ~50% surfaces rather than reusing one. Selection is hypothesized to concentrate around ≥2 concurrently in-flight objects/events, by analogy to the multi-worker concentration lesson. Held-out mutants (`receipt-order-trust`, `type-blind-keying`, `ledger-blind-resolve` — now one per surface) are run only against previously-unseen parameter combinations to confirm the selected region generalizes.
- **Cheapest package-validity probe:** Build the minimal consumer scaffold, the `platform_sim` sidecar (plus its `leaky-facade-canary` variant), the reference implementation, and the three narrow mutants (`dedupe-by-event-id`, `order-by-created`, `resend-closes-incident`, one per decision surface). Run one scenario containing an out-of-order pair, a split-duplicate pair, and a resend-during-active-retry case; confirm the reference passes, all three narrow mutants fail on genuinely distinct check lines, the always-refuse/no-op controls fail cleanly, and the `leaky-facade-canary` is detected as leaking. Also run the self-check-coverage predictor over the reference's own natural artifacts as a smoke test that the three `GradedRule` definitions actually fire. ~6-8 hours — the added canary and predictor checks reuse existing, validated code (`C8-witness-isolation-with-canary`, `src/screens/self-check-coverage.ts`) rather than new infrastructure, so the incremental cost over the pre-reconciliation ~4-6 hour estimate is small.
- **Three example instances:**
  1. Subscription lifecycle reconciliation (the family's calibration instance): subscription.updated/past_due/canceled events, a split-duplicate arising from a plan change that emits both an update and a delayed cancellation-superseding event pair, and a manual resend of a stuck renewal-failure webhook layered onto the platform's still-active daily retry cadence — exercising all three decision surfaces (identity/type collapse, freshness precedence, retry authority) jointly.
  1. Invoice/dispute reconciliation for a billing ledger: invoice.paid/invoice.payment_failed/charge.dispute.created events, a split-duplicate arising from one disputed charge emitting both a dispute-created and a funds-withdrawn event carrying overlapping dispute-state fields, and a dispute-unacknowledged SLA incident subject to the same manual-resend/auto-retry trap.
  1. Marketplace fulfillment webhook consumer: order.fulfilled/order.canceled/order.return_initiated events from a shipping platform, a split-duplicate arising from partial-shipment splitting (two ship-events for one logical fulfillment), and a carrier-scan-overdue SLA page that must not close on one manual re-ping while the carrier's own automated status-poll retry is still scheduled.
- **Estimated ordinary-to-recipe uplift:** Ordinary package ~15% → full-recipe ~35% (25-50%, low-to-medium confidence; not additive across operators). This revision does not raise the point estimate — there is still no MEASURED 0/N instantiation of this exact mechanism (evidenceType "fresh-external," isCalibrationAnchor false) — but it closes three concretely-identified epistemic gaps that would otherwise have independently predicted a VALID-BUT-EASY outcome by the CAA V1 precedent: a single-decision reference (now three independently-sized, independently-failable surfaces), no self-check-coverage signal (now a real, wired, non-blocking predictor), and an unproven isolation claim (now backed by a validated canary control). What improves is confidence that the FAIRNESS and DIFFICULTY claims underlying this number are checkable and defensible, not the number itself.
- **Main fairness/validity risks:** **Revision note (2026-09-04): reconciled against 3/3 REFUTED skeptic verdicts.** All three skeptics independently converged on the same four gaps, grounded in this repo's own Phase 17 CAA V1 postmortem. Rather than dispute them, this revision makes four concrete, checkable fixes:
1. Reference-implementation thinness (Phase 17 §12.3, "roughly fifteen lines with one decision") — fixed by explicitly budgeting and separating three independently-gradable decision surfaces (`outboxOperatorMapping` item 1/3), each with its own narrow mutant failing a distinct check line, replacing the prior draft's decoy "~1,000-1,200 line total service" metric.
2. Anti-pattern named in public text (Phase 17 §12.2) — confirmed present by all three skeptics in the prior `publicOutcomeRequirements` draft ("most recently created," "same event identifier," "operator-triggered resend"); removed and rewritten this revision (see `publicOutcomeRequirements`), with a new automated lint (`killConditions`) to prevent recurrence.
3. No real self-check-coverage predictor (Phase 17 §12.1) — was aspirational prose in the prior draft; this revision generalizes the existing `src/screens/self-check-coverage.ts` module into three family-specific `GradedRule`s wired as a non-blocking ranking feature on `ContractGateResult` (see `privateVerifierArchitecture`).
4. Unproven external-witness isolation claim (Phase 17 §12.5) — the prior draft cited container isolation as "now-standard" when reports/adversarial-container-isolation-report.md shows it is not operational anywhere in this repo; this revision claims only the validated `fs-sandbox` pattern and adds the existing `C8-witness-isolation-with-canary` negative control against this family's own sidecar (see `externalAuthorityOrWitness`).

Remaining risks, updated:
- The identity-plus-event-type keying rule must be fully derivable from the object/event schema genuinely shipped to the subject, not from information withheld only to the verifier — unchanged concern, now easier to check since `identity-type-collapse` is a named, separately-scoped decision surface with its own schema-derivability requirement.
- This project's own retired-candidates record (`payment-webhook-outbox-reconcile`, `duplicate-side-effect-webhook-retry`, both p≥0.85 in data/retired-candidates.json) flagged naive single-axis webhook-idempotency shapes as defaulting to "too easy." The three-surface structure and the requirement that graded scenarios jointly exercise all three (see `scenarioSelectionStrategy`) is the concrete mechanism meant to avoid repeating that history — this remains unmeasured until a campaign runs.
- The sidecar's automatic-retry schedule and delivery timing must be fully deterministic and frozen per scenario hash.
- The "operator-9-style" grade-from-counterparty's-record pattern (`grade-the-obligation-from-the-external-party-log` in data/hardness-operators.json) is correctly NOT credited as a hardness driver — that operator's own recorded evidence says it "did not measurably make the task hard," it is cited only as an architectural precedent for the ledger-based grading shape.
- Per Phase 17, the contract/verifier now records self-check-coverage per submission (not only pass/fail), so a clean solve rate cannot by itself mask a VALID-BUT-EASY outcome the way CAA V1's did — but this only holds once the predictor is actually wired in code, not merely specified here (see `killConditions`).
- **Kill conditions before paid trials:** - A consumer that dedupes solely on object id, ignoring event type and order entirely, nonetheless passes the full graded scenario set.
- A pure last-write-wins-by-receipt-order implementation passes any graded scenario.
- The `platform_sim` truth log or delivery/ack ledger proves reachable or inferable from inside the subject's own process under the `fs-sandbox` boundary actually in force (not the unbuilt container boundary).
- NEW: the `leaky-facade-canary` (the deliberately-leaky sidecar variant) is NOT detected as leaking by the isolation check — per Phase 17 §12.5, an isolation claim with no working negative control is not evidence, and this family may not proceed past discovery until the canary demonstrably trips.
- Grading is not reproducible across repeated runs at a fixed scenario hash.
- The cheapest-probe run cannot find a parameter setting where all three narrow mutants (`dedupe-by-event-id`, `order-by-created`, `resend-closes-incident`) each fail on their own distinct check line while the reference passes all three.
- NEW: the self-check-coverage predictor is not actually wired as a ranking field on `ContractGateResult` (i.e., remains aspirational text rather than shipped code) before this family is used to justify further build spend — per Phase 17 §12.1, a stated-but-unbuilt predictor does not close the VALID-BUT-EASY risk it is meant to address.
- NEW: an automated lint over the literal `publicOutcomeRequirements` text (extending `src/phase-16/contract-gate.ts`'s existing `FORBIDDEN_PHRASES` mechanism with family-specific tokens — `/\bevent\s*id\b/i`, `/\bcreated\b/i`, `/\breceipt order\b/i`, `/\bresend(ed)?\b/i`, `/\boperator[- ]?triggered\b/i`) finds any such token in the subject-visible instruction or schema text.
- Reviewers judge the manual-resend/incident-closure obligation an unrelated bolt-on rather than a natural expression of the same higher-level insight as the state-overwrite obligation; if it cannot be made to read as one mechanism across three surfaces, drop that facet rather than keep it as an unrelated second bug.
- **Adversarial verification:** 3/3 skeptics refuted; card was flagged and strengthened.

### Rollback That Reactivates a Dormant Defect (`rollback-reactivates-dormant-defect`)

- **Base family definition:** Responding to a live incident on one of several replicated deployment targets, the agent must diagnose root cause before acting, because rolling back a recent change without isolating the cause can reproduce an identical latent bug -- a repurposed flag/identifier reaching never-removed deprecated code -- on previously-unaffected replicas.
- **Durable-outbox operator mapping:** The literal append-only outbox is replaced by three domain-native external records that already exist in real fleet-ops/trading practice, per the instruction not to force outbox shape onto an unnatural family: (1) a venue/exchange execution ledger -- append-only, keyed by (parentOrderId, replicaId), recording what each replica actually submitted and what filled, independent of any replica's self-report; (2) a fleet attestation registry -- an independent infra service (not the subject's own status file) that fingerprints each replica's actually-running build hash and reachable-code signature on a schedule the subject does not control, checked against a deploy-target record fed only by the release pipeline (never by the subject's own writable deployment-manifest.json), so no remediation action can redefine what "intended version" means; (3) that same attestation service's own append-only inbound-query log, recording every request it receives -- including read-only audit calls -- with caller identity and timestamp. (3) is new in this revision, added specifically to close a gap an adversarial review identified: a build/reachability fingerprint alone attests to what CHANGED, not to what was merely READ/audited, so a subject's coverage claim ("I audited replicas 2, 5, 7") is now checked against this query log, not just fingerprint-change history. verifier-process-and-ledger-isolation: grading runs in a process the subject cannot import or patch, reading only these three external records plus a post-remediation canary probe, never the subject's own logs or "resolved" flag. a2-explicit-terminal-state-repair: one outcome sentence is stated explicitly and unambiguously -- "fleet convergence" means identical current-intended code (per the release-pipeline deploy-target record, not the subject-writable manifest) AND identical observable behavior for identical input across every replica, not merely "alerts have stopped" -- closing the ambiguity that let A2's terminal-state gap slip through undetected in the CAA lineage, and closing a second gap (uniform convergence onto a wrong, dormant-reactivating state passing as "resolved") by making no-retired-behavior an independent outcome requirement rather than relying on convergence alone. narrow-mutant-isolation: each dev/held-out mutant differs from the reference by exactly one policy switch, for attribution purposes only (guard the reused identifier vs. leave the dormant interpreter reachable; redeploy-and-restart the stale replica vs. roll the fleet back to match it) -- this is a pairwise reference-vs-one-mutant property and is distinct from, and does not substitute for, the reference-thickness-gate below, which governs the reference's own internal decision count. reference-thickness-gate (new this revision): the reference implementation is rejected at construction time unless it comprises at least four independently-necessary, separately-revertible changes, each mapped to a distinct verifier check that fails if that one change alone is reverted -- see cheapestPackageValidityProbe -- directly answering the Phase 17 postmortem finding that a ~15-line, one-decision reference let every agent's natural self-check cover the graded rule for free. b6-rig-integrity-controls: every rig run proves (a) the reference clears all replicas on the canary probe, (b) a targeted bad remediation (fleet-wide rollback) is caught reproducing the defect on every replica, (c) a probe run with a missing/unreachable ledger, attestation, or query-log feed voids rather than silently passes, and (d) a coverage-overclaim dev mutant (correct, narrowly-scoped remediation, but a submitted coverage report claiming replicas that were never queried) is caught by the self-check-coverage cross-check -- a demonstrated, tripped negative control, not merely an asserted capability, per the Phase 17 lesson that an isolation/non-leakage claim without a canary that can actually fail is not evidence. fuzz-controlling-parameter + recompute-activation-concentration: the fuzzer locates the parameter region (stale-replica presence x nonzero traffic carrying the colliding identifier value) that reliably activates the dormant path, runs explicit non-activation boundary controls (zero stale replicas; zero colliding-value traffic) to prove necessity, then concentrates scenario selection there rather than sampling broadly.
- **Non-applicable operators:** committed-idempotency-authority (literal form) -- this operator is about recovering a committed idempotency key across a crash/lease-epoch boundary for a single at-least-once tool call; this family's difficulty is about the SCOPE and diagnostic ordering of a multi-replica remediation, not about a single call surviving a crash mid-flight. No native lease-epoch/crash-recovery mechanic exists here, so it is not mapped; a weak idempotent-remediation analog (retrying a redeploy action must not double-apply or leave inconsistent per-replica state) is noted only as a plausible, unablated hypothesis, not a load-bearing operator. narrow-recompute-starter (literal form) -- the literal "recompute-from-current-state vs. recover-a-committed-value" axis doesn't exist in this family; the operator's general PATTERN (seed the visible starter with the natural, un-hinted, tempting shortcut) transfers by analogy via a default incident-response automation that already auto-triggers fleet-wide rollback above an error-rate threshold, but this is a different concrete mechanic and is listed under familyNativeDifficultyOperators rather than as a direct instantiation. recompute-activation-concentration (literal form) -- the literal multi-worker-recompute-race framing (n_workers, keys) does not apply; only the general CATEGORY (fuzz first, then concentrate scenario selection on the activating region) transfers, onto stale-replica-count/rollout-completion parameters instead of worker counts. Literal outbox/ledger structure (recipe point 5, in its DAO/CAA exactly-once-tool-call shape) -- not needed in that specific shape because the family's own domain already supplies a stronger, more natural external witness (venue execution ledger + independent build attestation + attestation query log); inventing an additional artificial internal ledger on top would be redundant and would violate the instruction against forcing outbox shape where the family has its own legitimate external witness.
- **Family-native difficulty operators:** Heterogeneous fleet runtime state that is invisible from a static manifest diff alone (status:"Complete" can be true and reachability can still differ) · a reused/repurposed identifier colliding with a never-removed deprecated interpreter, so the defect requires cross-referencing code history against per-replica build state rather than reading one file · rollback-as-harmful-remediation available as an in-authority, plausible, fast action with no visible warning attached to it · per-replica independent external authority, so no aggregate fleet-wide self-report can stand in for verifying each replica individually · a self-check-coverage requirement (the agent's own claimed audit/remediation scope is graded against the independent attestation fingerprint history AND its inbound-query log, not just pass/fail on "incident resolved") applying the Phase 17 CAA V1 postmortem lesson that pass/fail alone under-detects lucky or inconsistent remediations · a reference-thickness floor of four independently-necessary decisions (locate the specific deviant replica via attestation, not symptom guessing; structurally guard/remove the dormant path rather than route around it; redeploy AND restart -- confirmed via reachable-code fingerprint, not the manifest's version field; submit a coverage claim that matches the query log), applying the Phase 17 finding that a single-decision, ~15-line reference lets natural self-check cover the graded rule by accident · a design-time self-check-coverage predictor applied to this candidate (distinct from the runtime cross-check above, and added this revision in response to adversarial review): scored against the Phase 17 rubric of whether a generically-careful, non-diagnostic agent's ordinary incident-response self-check (redeploy the symptomatic node, watch alerts clear, glance at other replicas' status field) would incidentally satisfy the graded outcome. Verdict: LOW natural coverage -- a generic careful response naturally covers locating and redeploying the symptomatic replica, but has no organic reason to (i) requery independent attestation for every OTHER replica's reachable-code fingerprint rather than trusting alerts, (ii) verify restart-vs-redeploy-record-only via behavior rather than manifest version match, or (iii) constrain its own coverage claim to what it actually queried -- three of the four required decisions sit outside what ordinary carefulness alone would produce, unlike CAA V1 where 4/4 agents' natural rename-recheck habit covered the one graded rule for free. This is a reasoned, checkable, pre-registered judgment applied before construction spend, not yet a validated measurement -- a first pilot batch is the required next step to confirm it (see estimatedUplift and killConditions).
- **Multi-file codebase sketch:** A ~1,300-1,600 line order-execution fleet, fictional and originally authored (not a searchable upstream system): src/router/order-router.ts (parent-order intake; assigns a per-order attribute, e.g. affinityMode, for a currently-live smart-routing feature) · src/engine/order-engine.ts (core per-replica execution engine, imports a legacy interpreter module) · src/engine/legacy/accumulation.ts (a retired auto-accumulation fill-rebalancing path -- present, complete, and correct-looking, not marked WRONG/TODO, simply unreferenced by any currently-intended caller) · src/fleet/manifest.ts + data/deployment-manifest.json (per-replica build/version/rollout-status records the subject can act on, but which are independently re-attested rather than trusted at face value) · src/fleet/attestation-client.ts (client to an external, subject-unwritable attestation service) · src/venue/gateway-client.ts (client to an external venue simulator) · src/incident/monitor.ts (error-rate/alerting) · src/cli/remediate.ts (the agent's action surface: rollbackNode, rollbackFleet, redeployNode, quarantineNode, auditReachableCode, submitCoverageReport, markResolved) · CHANGELOG.md (states, as an ordinary historical fact, that the accumulation feature "was removed from routing logic in v2.3" -- true, and never flagged as relevant) · runbooks/incident-response.md (present only in some scenario variants -- the "written double-check procedure" dimension) · test/*.spec.ts (visible passing tests, see visibleTestStrategy). Outside the subject's editable tree and run only by the harness/verifier: venue-simulator/ (append-only receipt ledger); attestation-service/ (independent per-replica build+reachability fingerprint registry, plus -- added this revision -- its own append-only inbound-query log recording every request it receives, including read-only audit calls, used to check claimed-audit-scope against actually-queried-scope and close an audited-vs-changed gap identified in review); release-pipeline/ (deploy-target record stating each replica's current-intended build, fed only by the release process, never by the subject-writable deployment-manifest.json, so no remediation action can redefine what "intended version" means); canary-probe/ (post-remediation synthetic traffic injector).
- **Public outcome requirements:** Outcome-only, no defect location, mechanism, or forbidden-pattern name stated, and -- per this revision -- no general defensive strategy stated either. [Provenance note: a prior draft's requirement (4) read "any remediation action applied to additional replicas beyond the one exhibiting the incident must be justified by verified, replica-specific evidence." Three independent adversarial reviews flagged this as functionally equivalent to CAA V1's forbidden-outcomes leak -- it handed the agent the exact anti-pattern-avoidance heuristic ("verify each replica before acting broadly") the family is meant to test whether the agent discovers unprompted. On analysis the clause was also fully redundant for enforcement: a fleet-wide rollback that reactivates the dormant path on previously-healthy replicas already violates (1) below directly and behaviorally (via the canary probe), and rolling those replicas onto a stale build already violates (3) below (their code no longer matches the independently-tracked, subject-unwritable release-pipeline deploy-target record). The clause was therefore removed outright rather than reworded, closing the leak instead of arguing it was harmless.] (1) no replica may execute retired order-handling behavior, before or after remediation; (2) once remediation is complete, every replica must produce identical observable execution behavior for identical input, converged on code that independently matches the release pipeline's current-intended version -- not merely identical to each other (fleet convergence); (3) any replica whose currently-running code cannot be independently verified as matching the fleet's current intended version must be taken out of live order flow rather than left serving; (4) the incident must reach a fully resolved, live-serving state within the operational window -- indefinite refusal or escalation-only is not an acceptable terminal state; (5) the remediation's actual scope (which replicas were audited, changed, or left alone) must be independently reconstructable from the fleet's own attestation fingerprint history, attestation inbound-query log, and venue records, and any coverage the agent claims for itself is checked against those independent records rather than trusted.
- **Information intentionally absent from public instruction:** Which module or file contains the dormant/retired code path · that the routing attribute is a repurposed identifier shared with a retired feature · which replica (if any) is running a stale or incompletely-rolled-out build, and by how much · the fact that a fast, broad remediation exists that would make things categorically worse · any name for the historical incident or the general anti-pattern ("don't roll back blindly," "beware reused flags," etc. are never written into the instructions -- per the CAA V1 postmortem lesson, naming the pattern lets an agent avoid it by keyword-matching rather than by demonstrating the underlying diagnostic reasoning) · the exact number or identity of replicas that will carry the colliding traffic in the graded (held-out) scenario · the content of the written double-check runbook, even in scenario variants where the runbook artifact is present in-repo. This revision was additionally audited for accidental leaks of the general DEFENSIVE STRATEGY (not just the specific mechanism) into the public text -- distinguishing "state the graded rule" (fair) from "name the wrong implementation / hand over the avoidance heuristic" (not fair), per the Phase 17 postmortem's explicit framing. One such leak was found in an earlier draft's publicOutcomeRequirements and removed rather than reworded (see that field's provenance note); no other clause in the current text states a strategy, only outcomes and independently-checkable facts.
- **Visible-test strategy:** Existing, passing unit and integration tests cover ordinary operation on the current intended build: router assigns the routing attribute correctly for the live smart-routing feature, the engine executes and settles normal orders correctly, and the deployment-manifest schema validates. The realistic, source-supported gap: no test asserts that the retired accumulation path is UNREACHABLE (an absence-only property nobody thought to encode, because the router's own code no longer intentionally emits the old trigger value, so "removed from the router" was mistaken for "removed from the system"), and no test ever runs two differently-built replicas against shared traffic, so a multi-version fleet integration gap is never exercised. This mirrors a genuine, common test-suite blind spot (testing what code does, not what it must never again do) rather than a deliberately misleading test.
- **Private verifier architecture:** A separate verifier process, isolated from the subject's own service, reading only external state: (1) the venue execution ledger, cross-referenced against authorized order intents per replica; (2) the fleet attestation registry's independent build-hash and reachable-code fingerprint per replica, re-scanned after every remediation action rather than trusted from the manifest the subject can write, and checked against a release-pipeline deploy-target record the subject cannot edit (so no remediation can redefine "intended version"); (3) a canary-probe harness that, after remediation, injects synthetic tagged orders carrying the colliding identifier value at every replica and checks the venue ledger for any legacy-path signature, rather than trusting subject-side logs or a markResolved flag; (4) a self-check-coverage cross-check that compares the agent's own submitted account of what it audited/changed against BOTH the independent attestation fingerprint-change history AND the attestation service's own inbound-query log -- the query log is new this revision, added to close a gap where a fingerprint history alone can show what changed but not what was merely read/audited -- flagging mismatches as a distinct graded failure mode, not folded into a single pass/fail bit; (5) a construction-time reference-thickness gate, run once before any scenario is graded, that rejects the reference implementation unless it contains at least four independently-necessary, separately-revertible changes, each tied to a specific check above that fails if that one change is reverted alone -- directly applying the Phase 17 lesson that a single-decision reference lets natural self-check cover the graded rule for free. The verifier accepts any implementation strategy that achieves unreachability (deleting the dead module, namespacing the identifier, adding an explicit allow-list gate) -- it never checks for one specific diff shape.
- **External authority / witness:** Three independent, subject-unwritable records supply ground truth, chosen because they are this domain's own natural analogs rather than an invented ledger: the venue/exchange's append-only execution record (what each replica actually sent and what actually filled -- exchanges keep exactly this kind of record in real trading operations); an infrastructure fleet-attestation service that independently fingerprints each replica's running build and reachable code on its own schedule, checked against a release-pipeline deploy-target record the subject cannot edit (real fleets run exactly this kind of independent config/build-drift detector, separate from any service's self-reported health); and -- added this revision -- that same attestation service's own append-only inbound-query log, recording every request, including read-only audit calls, it receives (real fleet-attestation and secrets-management services commonly log every access for exactly this reason, and it closes the gap where a claimed audit, as opposed to a claimed change, had no independent record to check against). Each replica is independently authoritative for its own runtime state -- the verifier never accepts a fleet-wide aggregate or the subject's own status file as a substitute for per-replica evidence.
- **Fuzzer dimensions:** Replica count (3-12) · which replica index deviates, and by how much (never deployed / partially deployed but not restarted / fully stale) · share and volume of order traffic carrying the identifier's colliding value · venue acknowledgement latency and partial-fill timing · presence or absence of the written double-check runbook artifact · a concurrent unrelated decoy alert firing at the same time (noise control) · incident-detection lag · number of remediation actions already issued before verification begins (retry/idempotency stress on remediation actions) · presence/absence and accuracy of the agent's self-submitted coverage claim relative to what it actually queried and changed (the coverage-overclaim dimension, added this revision so the self-check-coverage cross-check's negative control is proven live, not just theoretically capable, in every rig run). Deterministic sweeps locate the controlling region (>=1 stale replica AND nonzero colliding traffic) and prove necessity with explicit non-activation controls (0 stale replicas; 0 colliding traffic) that must never trip the defect signature.
- **Narrow development mutants:** fleet-wide-rollback (rolls every replica back to the older build the instant the incident fires, without checking whether the others were already on the correct build) · node-quarantine-only (takes the originally affected replica out of rotation but leaves the shared engine's dormant path structurally unguarded, so it is never actually fixed) · manifest-trust-no-probe (accepts each replica's self-reported "status: current" field at face value and never queries the independent attestation/reachability service) · self-certified-resolution (marks the incident resolved once its own alerts clear, without any independent ledger or attestation confirmation) · coverage-overclaim (new this revision: performs the correct, narrowly-scoped remediation on only the genuinely deviant replica, but submits a coverage report additionally claiming to have audited replicas it never queried -- the required negative control proving the self-check-coverage cross-check can actually trip, per the Phase 17 requirement that every non-leakage claim ship a canary capable of failing).
- **Held-out mutants:** partial-rollback-inverted (rolls back only the currently-healthy replicas "to match" the affected one, reasoning they're now consistent, leaving the original replica still broken while newly breaking the rest) · redeploy-without-restart (issues a redeploy action and the manifest updates accordingly, but the running process is never restarted/reloaded, so the old code remains resident in memory despite the record showing convergence) · stale-attestation-race (redeploys correctly but certifies "all clear" by reading the attestation service before its next refresh cycle has landed, i.e. correct remediation paired with an unverified convergence claim).
- **Positive-work / cheat controls:** positive-work: the correct remediation (diagnose per-replica deviation via independent attestation, structurally guard/remove the dormant path, redeploy-and-restart the stale replica -- confirmed via reachable-code fingerprint, not manifest version -- confirm convergence via the independent probe, and submit an accurate coverage report) clears every replica on the canary probe and the coverage cross-check. no-op: doing nothing leaves the canary probe tripping on the originally affected replica -- fails. always-refuse: escalating without ever taking a remediation action leaves the incident open past the operational window -- fails. unconditional-success: calling markResolved without any logged remediation action or matching attestation change -- fails, since the verifier never reads that flag. hardcoding: a patch keyed to the literal originally-affected replica's id (e.g. "if replicaId==6, block") is defeated because held-out scenarios place the deviation on a different replica index. evidence-forging: a submitted coverage report claiming replicas were audited/redeployed that the independent attestation fingerprint history AND inbound-query log show were never touched or queried is caught by the self-check-coverage cross-check -- demonstrated live via the coverage-overclaim dev mutant (see narrowDevMutants), which must trip this check in every rig run as a required negative control, not merely a described capability. fleet-wide-rollback (the family's central trap, doubling as a cheat control): passes the originally affected replica's symptom but fails the canary probe on every previously-healthy replica once traffic carrying the colliding value reaches them.
- **Scenario-selection strategy:** Fuzz the full dimension grid, then select scenarios only from the region proven controlling: at least one replica genuinely stale (not merely flagged stale) AND nonzero order volume carrying the colliding identifier value reaching it. Require every graded scenario to place the deviation on a replica index not used during dev-mutant tuning, vary the runbook-presence and decoy-alert dimensions independently of the controlling parameters (so they cannot become an accidental proxy signal), and confirm via the zero-stale-replica and zero-colliding-traffic boundary controls that the defect never spuriously activates outside the identified region. Dev mutants are used only to tune this selection; held-out mutants are run once, unselected-against, for final validation. The coverage-overclaim dev mutant is additionally run as a mandatory canary in every rig cycle (dev and held-out both), because it must always trip the self-check-coverage cross-check; a rig cycle where it fails to trip voids that cycle's results rather than being scored, per the Phase 17 requirement that any non-leakage claim ship a canary capable of failing.
- **Cheapest package-validity probe:** Ship the fleet harness (N replica processes sharing the engine module, the venue simulator, the attestation service with both its fingerprint history and its inbound-query log, and the release-pipeline deploy-target record) with five remediation/audit actions wired: rollbackFleet(), redeployNode(id), auditReachableCode(id), quarantineNode(id), and submitCoverageReport(). In a scenario where the non-affected replicas are already on the correct, newer build, confirm (a) the fleet-wide-rollback narrow mutant is caught -- the post-remediation canary probe shows the dormant path now reachable on every replica, not just the original one; (b) the coverage-overclaim narrow mutant is caught -- a coverage report claiming unaudited replicas trips the self-check-coverage cross-check; (c) the reference remediation (redeploy the stale replica; guard the shared engine; restart confirmed via reachable-code fingerprint; accurate coverage report) clears every replica on the same probe; and (d) the reference diff itself spans at least three files and four independently-revertible hunks, each individually necessary -- reverting any one alone must reintroduce a specific, named probe failure -- and a reference that collapses to fewer voids the probe. Roughly 6-9 hours (revised up from the original 4-6 to account for wiring the two additional actions and running the thickness check).
- **Three example instances:**
  1. Equity order-execution fleet (the primary, Knight-Capital-adjacent instance): replicated order-routing/execution replicas; a repurposed order-attribute byte used by a live smart-routing feature collides with a retired auto-accumulation fill-rebalancing path that was never deleted, reachable only on a replica that silently missed a rollout restart.
  1. Card-payment authorization fleet: replicated authorization-decision services; a repurposed merchant-category override code collides with a retired manual-review-bypass path retained in a replica still running an older risk-engine build; a reflexive fleet-wide rollback would reopen the bypass on every replica that is currently blocking it correctly.
  1. Real-time market-data distribution fleet: replicated tick-normalization nodes; a repurposed subscription-tier identifier collides with a retired legacy tick-compression decoder on a node whose decoder upgrade silently failed to restart; here rollback is doubly wrong because the new decoder was itself the fix for a separate, unrelated compression defect the old decoder still carries.
- **Estimated ordinary-to-recipe uplift:** Directional estimate only, still no measured agent trial data for this exact family after this revision -- but now grounded in one additional, checkable piece of evidence beyond pure lineage analogy: the design-time self-check-coverage predictor (see familyNativeDifficultyOperators) scored this candidate LOW on natural self-check coverage, meaning a generically-careful but non-diagnostic agent's default incident response would NOT incidentally satisfy at least three of the four required decisions -- the same discriminator the Phase 17 postmortem identifies as separating CAA V1 (4/4 natural coverage, collapsed to easy) from the harder outbox lineage (5/6 no natural coverage). This is a reasoned pre-registration judgment, not a trial measurement, and could be wrong. Combined with removing the one identified public-text leak of the defensive strategy and raising the reference-implementation floor to four independently-necessary decisions (versus CAA V1's ~15-line, one-decision reference and this family's own earlier, thinner draft), the two concrete mechanisms most likely to have collapsed this family toward the "ordinary" band are now closed rather than merely argued against. Estimate: ordinary/shortcut-prone construction still risks ~50-60% (this floor has not changed -- nothing prevents a future re-draft from reintroducing a leak or a thin reference); full-recipe, as now specified, ~20-35% (same nominal band as before, now supported by the predictor's LOW-coverage finding rather than by analogy alone -- confidence raised from medium-low to medium, still short of a validated measurement). A first pilot batch of real trials, checked against this predictor's LOW-coverage claim and against the reference-thickness gate actually holding under construction, is the required next step before this band can be trusted; see killConditions for the specific miscalibration signal that would falsify it.
- **Main fairness/validity risks:** The stale replica's deviation must be discoverable through tools genuinely inside the agent's granted authority (attestation query, reachable-code audit, or reading that replica's actual running source) -- if diagnosis requires an ability not granted, the family becomes a guessing game rather than a fair diagnostic task. The rollback-everywhere trap must remain genuinely available and undisqualified by the interface (no visible warning attached to rollbackFleet()) or it has no teeth. A bounded pause-and-escalate response must not be punished as failure if the agent eventually completes remediation within the window -- real incident response legitimately involves pausing to verify, and treating caution itself as the failure would be unfair. The written double-check runbook, when present, must describe a general verification discipline and never spell out the specific fix, or its presence/absence becomes the only real source of difficulty and the family collapses to one binary switch. The verifier must accept any implementation strategy that achieves unreachability rather than prescribing one code shape. Resolved this revision, not merely argued away: (a) publicOutcomeRequirements previously stated the general defensive heuristic ("justify additional-replica action with per-replica evidence") in words -- three independent adversarial reviews flagged this as functionally equivalent to CAA V1's forbidden-outcomes leak; the clause was removed as redundant rather than reworded, closing the leak instead of defending it. (b) the self-check-coverage cross-check previously had no demonstrated negative control and no way to distinguish "audited" from "changed" -- both closed by adding the attestation service's inbound-query log and the mandatory coverage-overclaim canary mutant. Residual, still-open risk, stated plainly: the reference-thickness gate and the self-check-coverage predictor are both construction-time judgments applied by the recipe's designers, not yet validated against real agent trials -- if a first pilot batch shows a generically-cautious mutant clearing the full scenario set despite the LOW-coverage predictor verdict, the predictor itself is miscalibrated for this family and the card needs re-auditing (see killConditions).
- **Kill conditions before paid trials:** The dormant path's reachability cannot be established through any action or read available to the agent within its authority boundary (defect becomes unknowable rather than non-obvious) · a reflexive "never roll back, always redeploy-and-restart, always audit first" heuristic -- applied with no actual per-replica diagnosis -- passes the full graded scenario set, meaning the family collapsed to a keyword shortcut rather than testing causal reasoning; this is now specifically monitored via the coverage-overclaim canary and the four-decision reference-thickness gate rather than merely hoped against, but a first pilot batch showing this pattern despite the design-time predictor's LOW-coverage verdict would mean the predictor is miscalibrated for this family and construction should pause for re-audit · the fleet-wide-rollback narrow mutant still passes because graded scenarios never actually route colliding traffic to the previously-healthy replicas post-remediation (a scenario-selection defect, but fatal to the current instantiation) · the subject's own process retains write access to the attestation registry, venue ledger, or the attestation service's inbound-query log, letting it self-certify convergence or fabricate an audit trail · the attestation service's inbound-query log can be bypassed, spoofed, or is reachable through a channel the verifier does not read, letting a subject claim an audit it never performed -- this would silently defeat the newly-added audited-vs-changed check and must void, not pass, affected scenarios · blind reviewers judge the reused-identifier-collides-with-dormant-code mechanism as requiring memorized incident trivia rather than demonstrable engineering diagnosis from the artifacts actually shipped.
- **Adversarial verification:** 3/3 skeptics refuted; card was flagged and strengthened.

### CDN and DNS Cache Staleness Verification (`cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap`)

- **Base family definition:** Unchanged from the input family record (none of the three skeptic verdicts attacked the family definition itself, only the construction quality around it). Definition: "Agent makes an authoritative change at an origin system (a DNS record or a CDN-fronted asset) and must correctly verify propagation despite intermediate caching layers that are contractually permitted to serve stale data until TTL- or purge-type-specific windows lapse, choosing the correct purge type and multi-tier verification sequence rather than treating one re-fetch as proof." Task shape: update an origin resource, issue a purge (soft/hard/tag/prefix) against a simulated multi-tier CDN and/or update a DNS record, under harness-enforced stale-serve windows, tier-revalidation semantics (EXPIRED vs MISS), and purge rate limits; the agent must choose a verification sequence that correctly distinguishes legally-stale from genuinely-failed propagation. Causal axis: stale-authority-replay-against-changed-state. Authority boundary: the origin (zone or origin server) is sole source of truth; caches hold delegated, time-bounded copies whose staleness is contractually legal, so authority to declare content live is bounded by confirming propagation at the correct tier, not by absence of an API error. Subject-inaccessible witness: the agent cannot inspect cache-node internal state directly, only whatever headers/values a probed tier returns, with no built-in freshness signal unless it deliberately applies documented status semantics. Natural scenario dimensions: TTL length; purge type (hard/soft/tag/prefix); single- vs multi-tier topology; plan-tier rate limits; verification method chosen. Why agents may fail: a single purge-then-refetch will, per documented semantics, still legitimately see stale content (false FAILED) or accept a still-caching tier's EXPIRED response as proof of a true MISS (false LIVE). Why fair: staleness rules (RFC 2181 TTL semantics, documented CDN purge-type behavior) are public, stable, and derivable from specification/vendor docs, requiring correct application of a documented cache contract rather than hidden internal knowledge. None of these clauses are altered by the construction recipe below; the recipe only supplies how a repairable, gradeable, cheat-resistant package gets built around this definition.
- **Durable-outbox operator mapping:** RECONCILIATION NOTE (all three skeptic passes returned refuted=true): the panel converged on the same four concrete, textually-grounded gaps — (1) the planted defect was two ANDed sub-clauses folded into one loop, which is exactly the collapsible "one decision" shape the CAA postmortem (reports/PHASE-17-CAA-VALIDATION.md) warned against; (2) publicOutcomeRequirements enumerated three "must never X/Y/Z" clauses that map ~1:1 onto five of the six plausible-mistake mutants, functioning as a near-answer-key; (3) the only "self-check-coverage predictor" on offer was a future promise to run a few live solves and eyeball pass/fail — the same shape as the CAA smoke campaign this recipe is reacting against, not the structural, pre-trial predictor the postmortem's recommendation #1 actually asked for; (4) no mutant existed to prove the identity-preserving witness cross-check has a reachable failing branch, the same unfalsifiable-isolation-claim gap the postmortem found in two of CAA's seven Phase 16 controls. Per instruction, these are accepted and fixed structurally below (items 3 and 4, plus the witness clause, publicOutcomeRequirements, and scenarioSelectionStrategy), not re-argued.

Mapping is against the 8 operators named in the prompt (data/hardness-operators.json) plus one additional measured operator found live in that file that is unusually on-point for this family (grade-the-obligation-from-the-external-party-log); the extra one is flagged as a bonus, not substituted for any of the 8.

1. verifier-process-and-ledger-isolation (validity-control) -> The CDN tier-state store, the DNS zone/resolver-cache state, and the purge control-plane (purge acceptance, blast-radius computation, rate-limit counters) live in a root-owned service the submitted code never imports and cannot mutate directly; the grading harness runs in a separate unprivileged process from scenario execution. The submitted `publishAndVerify()` only ever sees simulated probe responses over a fixed interface, never the tier/resolver state objects themselves. The "ledger" is not one append-only call ledger but three cooperating root-owned stores (purge-ack log, tier-state timeline, probe-access log) — see externalAuthorityOrWitness. NEW: two of the three stores now additionally carry a registered negative-control canary (narrowDevMutants 11-12, plus heldOutMutants 5) proving the isolation boundary has a reachable failing branch — the direct repair for the exact gap Phase 17 found in CAA's `witnessIsolated`/`challengeNonleakage` checks, both unfalsifiable as constructed there.

2. a2-explicit-terminal-state-repair (validity-control) -> The public contract states, as a positive definition rather than a prohibition, that a purge id's committed acceptance timestamp and coverage plan are the sole baseline a report is ever evaluated against, regardless of how many rate-limit-forced retries occur for that id; and that a tier's stale-while-revalidate allowance is bounded per content-version rather than renewing on repeated legitimate stale hits. (Previously phrased partly as "must not restart the window" — rewritten below into publicOutcomeRequirements as a closed definition, per the point-2 fix.)

3. narrow-mutant-isolation (validity-control) -> MATERIALLY REWRITTEN, this is the direct fix for skeptic finding (1). The prior construction planted a single flawed exit condition folded into one loop with two ANDed sub-clauses (blast-radius coverage AND version match) — exactly the shape flagged as "plausibly still a single compound condition... the kind of thing a competent agent's ordinary self-check would produce spontaneously." That risk is accepted as valid and repaired structurally, not argued away: the two sub-clauses are now split into two separate, independently unit-testable functions in two separate files — `planPurgeAndCoverage()` in `purgePlanner.ts` (a static, pre-dispatch decision: given change metadata, which tiers does the chosen purge type actually reach) and `isTierConverged()` in `convergenceJudge.ts` (a runtime, per-probe decision: does this tier's returned content match the new version). Two new intermediate mutants (narrowDevMutants 2 and 3) each correctly fix exactly one function while leaving the other's flaw in place, and are registered in advance to activate on non-overlapping fixture subsets — mutant 2 (coverage-complete-but-status-only) fails only where every planned tier is probed but one holds a stale version; mutant 3 (version-checked-but-single-tier-only) fails only where the coverage plan itself is incomplete. See scenarioSelectionStrategy for the exact partition and killConditions for the concrete kill trigger if the two are not actually separable on the fuzz grid. This turns "the reference is thicker than CAA's ~15-line/one-decision fix" from an assertion into a falsifiable, pre-registered claim with its own kill condition.

4. b6-rig-integrity-controls (validity-control) -> Mandatory in every grading invocation: the reference must pass, each narrow mutant (now 12, up from 8) must fail exactly its intended check(s) on activating fixtures and pass on control fixtures, and a malformed or missing probe-access-log/control-plane record must raise VOID rather than return a verdict. NEW, this is the direct fix for skeptic finding (4): two registered evidence-forging canaries (narrowDevMutants 11 fabricated-tier-citation, 12 replayed-stale-probe-citation) must each be caught by the identity-preserving cross-check, and a third, disjoint, held-out canary (heldOutMutants 5, forged-future-probe-timestamp) must be caught without having been used to build the check — closing the exact C8-shaped gap (an isolation/non-leakage claim with no reachable failing branch) Phase 17 found in two of CAA's seven reported Phase 16 controls.

5. committed-idempotency-authority (difficulty) -> Family-native form: "committed-purge-window-authority," unchanged in mechanism: a correct implementation persists and later recovers the control-plane's own committed purge-acceptance timestamp and computed blast radius across rate-limit-forced retries, rather than recomputing "the window starts now." This machinery is read by, but lives outside, both `purgePlanner.ts` and `convergenceJudge.ts` — it is preserved-correct machinery the agent must not break while repairing the two split decisions above.

6. narrow-recompute-starter (difficulty) -> The visible starter implements the tempting, plausible-looking "stop once any tier is non-MISS, no coverage check, no version check, recompute wait-window from local dispatch time" policy in full working order (this is now explicitly narrowDevMutants 1 plus narrowDevMutants 6's recompute-window flaw, shipped together as the starting point), leaving both split repairs to the agent.

7. fuzz-controlling-parameter (scenario-selection) -> Deterministic fuzzing grid, now crossing coverage-plan correctness and version-match correctness independently (see fuzzerDimensions) so mutants 2 and 3's activation regions can be shown disjoint rather than assumed disjoint.

8. recompute-activation-concentration (scenario-selection) -> Scenario selection concentrated on the multi-tier, partial-blast-radius region, with explicit single-tier and full-blast-radius-purge controls required to show zero activation for every conditional mutant, including the new pair (2, 3).

Bonus: grade-the-obligation-from-the-external-party-log -> The requirement "verification must cover every tier in the purge's actual blast radius, in a legitimate order, without exceeding rate limits" is graded against the harness's own root-owned, append-only probe-access log and the control-plane's own blast-radius record, never against subject self-report — now with the reachable-failing-branch canaries described in item 4 above.
- **Non-applicable operators:** Unchanged in substance from the input card. None of the 8 measured operators are wholesale inapplicable to this family — each has a family-native translation, listed above. What does not transfer is not an operator but a literal structural default from the outbox/CAA lineage: (a) a single append-only *effect/call* ledger — replaced by three cooperating stores, now with registered canaries proving they are non-forgeable rather than merely claimed non-forgeable; (b) a crash/lease-epoch framing for committed-idempotency-authority — the natural trigger here is rate-limit-forced retry and asynchronous propagation delay, not process failure. The new two-function split (purgePlanner.ts / convergenceJudge.ts) does not reintroduce a literal outbox ledger structure — it is still native to this family's own purge/coverage vocabulary, not copied from the outbox's payment-ledger shape.
- **Family-native difficulty operators:** Proposed, family-native, currently structurally-separated-but-still-agent-unmeasured (hypotheses; a hand-scored structural predictor now exists, see scenarioSelectionStrategy, but no agent has yet attempted this package):

- committed-purge-window-authority: recover the control plane's committed purge-ack timestamp and coverage-plan record across rate-limit-forced retries, instead of recomputing "window starts now." Unchanged from the prior card; already-correct, preserved machinery the agent must read and not break.
- blast-radius-scoped-tier-coverage: now implemented as its own function, `planPurgeAndCoverage()` in `purgePlanner.ts` — a purge-type/tier-set mismatch (e.g. a tag purge that never reaches the shield tier for an asset whose tag mapping changed) must be caught by coverage, not just by the version check below. This is no longer one ANDed clause inside the polling loop; it is a separate, independently swappable decision, with its own intermediate mutant (narrowDevMutants 2) proving it is separately gradeable.
- version-confirmed-convergence: now implemented as its own function, `isTierConverged()` in `convergenceJudge.ts` — a tier's cache-status code alone is not proof of the new content; the returned content's version marker must match the origin's new value. Separately gradeable via narrowDevMutants 3.
- rate-limit-respecting bounded retry: unchanged — verification must neither exceed the documented rate limit nor treat a 429 as evidence of staleness.
- negative-caching-aware DNS bound (DNS-side instances only): unchanged — RFC 2308 negative-caching plus RFC 2181 positive TTL, sampled across an independently-aging resolver population.
