forwards calls, records the append-only ledger (call id, turn, server, tool, input, output, timestamps, placeholder-flag), never exposes the ledger to the agent; ships a leaky-facade build variant used only by the isolation-audit CI, never by real agents. -- scenario-generator/: builds one scenario instance by sampling catalog composition (real + decoy servers/tools), a target dependency DAG (depth, branch width, join points), and a fuzzy natural-language request consistent with that DAG; emits the frozen, hashed reference DAG and per-instance backing-data snapshot alongside the instance. -- contract-gate/: runs the generic-diligence-probe baseline against each candidate instance pre-trial, computes the per-axis self-check-coverage score, and rejects or reweights instances/axes a generically diligent, family-uncoached agent already clears for free. -- reference-solver/: a hand-written policy that discovers and threads correctly, used only for validity probes and as the positive-work control, never shipped to the agent. -- narrow-mutants/: keyword-router, serial-only, eager-placeholder, over-fetch-everything -- single-policy-switch baselines used to prove each scenario activates the axis it's meant to test. -- held-out-mutants/: narrator, half-chain -- run only at validation time, never used for scenario selection. -- verifier/: isolated process; ledger-replay + independent backing-data recompute + LLM claim-extraction judge; emits a decomposed score (discovery precision/recall, dependency-order violations, missed-parallelism count, claim-coverage ratio), never a bare pass/fail. -- isolation-audit/: runs the leaky-facade canary against the gateway before any real build ships. -- harness/: freezes and hashes the catalog/DAG/dataset per instance, launches agent/gateway/verifier in separated containers, captures all artifacts durably for blind labelling.
- **Public outcome requirements:** Answer the business question actually asked. -- Every factual claim, computed figure, or recommendation in your final deliverable must be derived from the actual output of a tool you successfully called during this session -- never from an assumed, remembered, estimated, or placeholder value. -- You have access to a shared catalog of tools spanning multiple independently-run systems. -- If the request genuinely cannot be completed with the tools available, say so plainly rather than presenting an incomplete or estimated answer as if it were complete.

(Revision note: an earlier draft additionally stated "wait for it rather than substituting a typical or estimated value and correcting it later," "prefer requesting them independently rather than strictly one at a time," and "some may look related to what you need without actually serving that purpose -- confirm what a tool actually takes and returns before relying on it." All three sentences named, in words, the exact mechanisms this family's placeholder, parallelism, and decoy-discovery axes are meant to test -- reproducing CAA V1's documented failure mode. They are removed. The surviving text states only the terminal outcome rule, matching this card's own stated design intent for the first time.)
- **Information intentionally absent from public instruction:** The identity of the load-bearing tool subset and servers for this specific request; the correct call sequence and which pairs of calls are truly independent versus share a hidden shared dependency; which catalog entries are decoys versus which merely look unfamiliar; the number of hops required; the exact figures/answer; the specific contract defect (unit, granularity, staleness) carried by any near-miss decoy tool for this instance; and (new) the generic-diligence-probe's per-axis self-check-coverage score for a given instance, which is computed and used only at the contract gate and is never surfaced to the agent, in the public instructions, or in any per-trial report the agent could see.
- **Visible-test strategy:** Each server ships its own passing per-tool smoke test: call the tool once with a canonical input, assert the expected output shape. This is realistic -- it is exactly how a tool-server SDK is unit-tested -- and it is also exactly where the coverage gap sits: a per-endpoint test can never demonstrate a cross-server chain, so nothing visible shows the agent what correct multi-hop threading looks like, and nothing visible reveals which hops are load-bearing. The public instructions no longer supplement this gap with mechanism-level hints (see publicOutcomeRequirements), so the gap is now genuinely load-bearing rather than partially closed by the contract text.
- **Private verifier architecture:** Two distinct mechanisms, run at two distinct times, not one conflated with the other.

(0) Pre-trial self-check-coverage predictor (contract-gate ranking feature, not a post-hoc score): before a candidate scenario instance is admitted to the paid-trial pool, it is run once against a generic-diligence-probe baseline -- an agent instructed with nothing but the scrubbed publicOutcomeRequirements and ordinary professional tool-use hygiene (check a schema before trusting it, don't act on a value you don't have yet, look for independent work), with zero family-specific coaching about discovery, decoys, ordering, or parallelism. Per graded axis (discovery precision, placeholder-avoidance, missed-parallelism), record whether this baseline already clears it. Any axis the generic-diligence baseline clears at or near ceiling is dropped from that instance's scored set, or the instance is rejected outright. Only instances where clearing an axis demonstrably requires the family's actual target skill -- not baseline professional caution alone -- are retained. This is the concrete implementation of the CAA V1 postmortem's actual lesson ("a self-check-coverage predictor as a ranking feature at the contract gate," not a richer post-hoc rubric), and it is the mechanism that would have caught this card's earlier, over-explicit publicOutcomeRequirements before any trial was funded.

(1) Post-trial isolated verifier process, never trusting the agent's self-reported completion state: it replays the harness-owned ledger to reconstruct the realized call graph and diffs it against the frozen reference dependency DAG for dependency-order violations and missed-parallelism; it independently re-queries each server's own backing dataset for ground-truth values rather than accepting the agent's quoted tool output, catching evidence-forging even when a cited call id is real but its reported value is misstated; it runs an LLM-judge claim-extraction pass over the final deliverable, mapping each assertion to a ledger-confirmed call and value; and it reports a decomposed score -- discovery precision/recall, dependency-order violation count, missed-parallelism count, claim-coverage ratio -- rather than a single pass/fail, so partial capability is visible. This decomposed score is a richer post-hoc rubric and is explicitly not the self-check-coverage predictor; the two are separate mitigations for separate postmortem lessons and both are now present.
- **External authority / witness:** A harness-owned trajectory gateway is the only path between agent and servers and writes an append-only ledger (call id, server, tool, input, output, timestamps, placeholder-flag) the agent can never read back or rewrite -- the family's natural analog of an outbox ledger, since no individual server can see whether its output was consumed correctly downstream. Each server's backing dataset is independently authoritative for its own domain and is queried directly by the verifier. The reference dependency DAG for a scenario instance is derived mechanically by the scenario generator from the servers' actual declared contracts, not hand-authored per instance and not equivalent to any one reference agent's trajectory -- so it is an objective ground truth the agent cannot access or influence.

New, falsifiable non-leakage control (per this project's own C8-witness-isolation-with-canary precedent, added because two independent skeptics found the original design asserted but never demonstrated non-leakage): before the real gateway is approved for any paid trial, a deliberately leaky-facade build is run through the isolation audit -- this build intentionally leaks one bit of ledger-derived data into an agent-visible channel (e.g. echoing call-order metadata or the placeholder-flag into an error message or response envelope). The isolation audit must detect and flag this leaky-facade run. If the canary is not caught, the non-leakage claim for the real gateway is treated as unverified and the family cannot ship, regardless of how clean the real gateway's own logs look -- exactly the fix for controls that previously "could not fail."
- **Fuzzer dimensions:** Number of servers/tools exposed in this instance's catalog slice (including decoy servers) -- dependency-chain depth (2-hop through 4-5-hop) -- degree of legitimate parallelism (branch width and join points in the true DAG) -- instruction fuzziness (how purely business-language versus domain-suggestive the request is) -- count and placement of near-miss decoy tools -- per-server response latency variance (so serialization has a real, not merely notional, cost) -- whether intermediate values are naturally re-fetchable or must be reused once obtained.
- **Narrow development mutants:** keyword-router: selects tools by string-matching request words to tool names/descriptions, ignoring documented schemas -- fails discovery once the request is paraphrased away from tool nomenclature. -- serial-only: discovers and threads correctly but always executes one call at a time in discovery order, never concurrently -- stays correct but should score low on the parallelism sub-metric, isolating that axis from correctness. -- eager-placeholder: issues a downstream call using a guessed or typical value before the upstream call it depends on has returned, then reconciles afterward -- must register as a dependency-order violation regardless of whether the eventual numeric answer is close; this axis is now genuinely live since the public text no longer describes the wait-vs-guess mechanic. -- over-fetch-everything: calls every tool in every server "to be safe," then discards irrelevant outputs -- stays claim-covered but should be penalized on discovery precision so brute-force exhaustiveness cannot substitute for genuine discovery. -- generic-diligence-probe (contract-gate only, not a graded mutant): an agent coached with nothing but the scrubbed public instructions and ordinary professional caution, no family-specific hints -- used exclusively pre-trial to compute the self-check-coverage predictor; an instance/axis it clears at ceiling is rejected before any real trial is funded.
- **Held-out mutants:** narrator: produces a fluent, confident final report citing plausible figures with no underlying tool calls at all -- must score zero on claim coverage (the evidence-forging control, held out to validate the LLM judge isn't fooled by fluent unsupported prose). -- half-chain: correctly executes the first portion of the dependency chain, then fabricates the remainder once it runs out of the obvious next steps -- validates that claim coverage is checked at the level of individual assertions, not just whether the overall report "sounds complete."
- **Positive-work / cheat controls:** positive-work: the reference solver executes the full correct trajectory, every claim ledger-covered, correct ordering, legitimate branches run concurrently -- scores at ceiling. -- no-op / always-refuse: declines to call tools or claims the task is impossible when it is genuinely answerable -- scores zero. -- unconditional-success / hardcoded-answer: returns a plausible generic figure typical for the domain without a matching, scenario-specific tool trajectory -- caught because backing-dataset values are refreshed per instance, so a hardcoded number will not match the independently re-queried ground truth. -- evidence-forging: final deliverable cites a call or value that the ledger shows never happened, or misstates a value the ledger shows was actually returned -- caught by ledger cross-check regardless of how the call is cited. -- over-fetch-everything: calling every available tool regardless of relevance is not accepted as equivalent to genuine discovery -- graded down on discovery precision even when claim coverage is technically complete. -- leaky-facade canary (infrastructure-side, new): a deliberately compromised gateway build that leaks one bit of ledger-derived data into an agent-visible channel must be caught by the isolation audit -- a fixed pre-ship scan for ledger-derived fields appearing in any agent-visible payload -- before the real gateway is approved for a paid trial; this is distinct from and complementary to the agent-side evidence-forging control, closing the "isolation claim with no negative control" gap.
- **Scenario-selection strategy:** Generate candidate instances by sampling catalog composition (real + decoy servers/tools), target DAG shape (depth, branch width, join points), and a fuzzy request consistent with that DAG. Run all narrow dev mutants against each candidate and keep only instances where: the reference passes with full claim coverage and zero violations; keyword-router fails discovery; serial-only passes correctness but is measurably penalized on the parallelism sub-score on every instance selected for that axis; eager-placeholder is reliably caught as a dependency-order violation; and single-branch control instances show no parallelism penalty for any correct trajectory, serial or not. Discard instances solvable by keyword-router, or where serial and parallel trajectories are scored identically.

New gate, run before an instance enters the paid-trial pool: run the generic-diligence-probe baseline (scrubbed public instructions plus ordinary professional tool-use caution only, no family coaching) against the candidate and score, per graded axis, whether this baseline already clears it without genuine multi-hop skill. Retain only instances/axes where the generic-diligence baseline still fails -- i.e. where clearing the axis demonstrably requires the family's actual target skill rather than baseline caution alone. This is the pre-registration ranking predictor the CAA V1 postmortem calls for, and it is the mechanism that would have flagged this card's own earlier over-explicit instructions before any trial was funded.

Validate the surviving instance set against the held-out mutants (narrator, half-chain) to confirm the claim-coverage/evidence-forging checks generalize beyond the mutants used for selection, and against the leaky-facade canary to confirm the isolation boundary is falsifiable before any instance is run for real.
- **Cheapest package-validity probe:** Build a minimal 3-server slice (~12-15 tools) with the trajectory gateway, ledger, verifier, the reference solver, two narrow-mutant baselines (keyword-router, serial-only), the generic-diligence-probe baseline, and a leaky-facade canary build of the gateway. Run it against ~5 hand-built scenario instances. Confirm: (a) the reference reaches full claim coverage and zero violations on all 5; (b) keyword-router fails discovery on at least 3/5; (c) serial-only passes correctness on all 5 but scores measurably lower than a genuinely parallel correct trajectory on the >=2 instances that have a real parallel branch; (d) the generic-diligence-probe, using only the scrubbed public instructions, fails to clear discovery-precision, placeholder-avoidance, and parallelism on the instances selected for those axes (proving the instructions no longer pre-solve the task); (e) the isolation audit catches the leaky-facade canary build. If any of these fail, redesign before building out the full dozens-of-tools catalog and the decoy set. Estimated ~2.5-3.5 engineering days (added ~0.5 day versus the original probe for the two new gates).
- **Three example instances:**
  1. Retail Ops and Fulfillment Analyst -- ~7 servers/~40 tools (CRM, inventory/warehouse, shipping-carrier rates, marketing/promo, finance/pricing, plus 2 wholly-irrelevant decoy servers such as HR and facilities) and 3 near-miss decoy tools including a stale 'get_shipping_quote_legacy'. Request: 'Our biggest Pacific-Northwest customer wants their standing order out before their store's reopening event -- can we make it happen, and what would it cost us?' Correct chain: CRM lookup (customer, region, order/SKUs) -> inventory availability at the matching warehouse (needs the SKU list and region from CRM) -> two independent branches once warehouse and SKUs are known -- a shipping-rate/ETA query to the customer's address, and a promo/discount-eligibility check for the customer's tier -- which the finance/pricing tool then merges into a landed cost. Depth 3 with a width-2 parallel branch.
  1. Clinical Operations and Staffing Analyst -- ~9 servers (patient-scheduling, staffing-roster, equipment-inventory, billing-eligibility, facilities-maintenance, plus generic marketing/HR decoys). Request: 'Can we add a second MRI slot Thursday afternoon without pulling a tech from the ER rotation, and will insurance actually cover it for the patients on the waitlist?' Correct chain: scheduling returns the waitlist and the specific MRI unit id; staffing-roster (independent of scheduling's waitlist) finds techs not on ER rotation; equipment-inventory needs the unit id from scheduling to confirm it isn't in maintenance; billing-eligibility needs each waitlisted patient's id and plan (a fan-out of independent per-patient calls) to check coverage. Tests whether the agent recognizes N independent per-patient calls it can parallelize rather than looping them serially by habit.
  1. Field-Service and Warranty Analyst -- ~8 servers (asset-registry, warranty-terms, parts-inventory, technician-dispatch, customer-contract, plus recruiting/expense-report decoys), including a near-miss decoy tool 'warranty_check_v2' from a different legacy program with a different terms table. Request: 'A customer says their compressor tripped again -- is it still under warranty, what part likely needs replacing based on the fault-code history, do we have it in stock nearby, and get a tech scheduled if so.' Correct chain: asset-registry lookup by customer/site (no asset id given) -> fault-code history (needs the asset id) -> two independent branches -- warranty-terms (needs install date and fault category) and parts-inventory (needs the inferred part SKU and region) -- that must both resolve before a conditional final dispatch call, which must never fire if either branch fails or before both have returned. Depth 4 with a real join dependency and a conditional final hop.
- **Estimated ordinary-to-recipe uplift:** Fresh-external evidence (foundry-mcp-bench) already reports dependency-awareness/parallelism scores rarely exceeding ~0.30, degrading further as server count grows -- so the base difficulty finding is externally supported, not this project's invention. This recipe's own contribution is validity, not new difficulty: an ordinary version of this task (score correctness only, self-reported completion, no ledger, no decoys, no cheat suite) likely reads as solvable ~45-55% of the time by a capable agent, but that number is largely uninformative because nothing stops hardcoded/forged-evidence answers or rewards a brute-force call-everything strategy. Once the harness-owned ledger, independent backing-data recompute, discovery-precision penalty, dependency-order/parallelism scoring, generic-diligence-probe gate, and leaky-facade canary are added and cheat-proofed, a genuinely joint correctness-and-planning-quality bar plausibly drops measured success into a ~15-30% band, consistent with, not independently re-deriving, the cited external ~0.30 ceiling.

Revision note: the card's earlier draft carried this same band while its public instructions were still doing part of the agent's discovery/ordering/parallelism work for it -- meaning that draft's eventual measured number, had it been run, was at real risk of landing above this band for reasons that would have had nothing to do with genuine capability (an optimistic bias in the same direction as CAA V1's). With the mechanism-level hints removed and the generic-diligence-probe gate screening out any instance a merely-careful baseline would already clear, the 15-30% band is retained as the target but should now be read as the post-scrub, post-gating estimate. Confidence: medium-low; this instantiation is unbuilt and the number is a hypothesis pending the validity probe, which now must also confirm (d) and (e) above before the estimate is trusted.
- **Main fairness/validity risks:** Decoys must differ in real substantive contract (wrong region, stale version, coarser granularity), never in a purely cosmetic naming trick -- a decoy that's undiscoverable except by luck is a puzzle, not a fair analyst task. -- The request's fuzziness must reflect genuine natural business-language ambiguity a real analyst would face, not information withheld to the point that no single correct tool subset exists. -- Parallelism must be graded as a separate planning-quality dimension, never folded into correctness, so a capable-but-conservative serial agent is not scored as if it failed the task. -- The LLM claim-coverage judge must be validated against the held-out narrator/half-chain mutants so it isn't fooled by fluent unsupported prose, and must not falsely flag legitimate paraphrase or rounding as unsupported. -- Backing datasets must vary meaningfully per scenario instance so memorized 'typical' industry figures cannot accidentally pass. -- Per the CAA V1 postmortem, the correct trajectory must require multiple genuine judgment points, not collapse to one obvious 'aha' call. -- Public instructions must state the terminal outcome rule only and must never name, paraphrase, or walk through any graded mechanism in words (fixed in this revision after three independent skeptics found the prior draft did exactly this for the placeholder, parallelism, and decoy axes); any future edit to publicOutcomeRequirements should be re-run through the generic-diligence-probe gate before being trusted. -- The generic-diligence-probe itself needs its own calibration: if it is defined too strictly it will reject valid instances, and if defined too loosely it will pass instances the original text-scrub problem still afflicts, so its own pass/fail thresholds should be spot-checked by a human reviewer during the validity probe, not accepted as self-certifying. -- The leaky-facade canary must actually be caught by the isolation audit, not merely declared as a control that exists; per the C8 precedent this needs to be demonstrated with a real detector trip, not asserted. -- Because the source family is a real named external benchmark (foundry-mcp-bench), the constructed servers, datasets, and scenarios must be original fictional designs inspired by the family's shape, never copied or closely paraphrased from that benchmark's actual public tool/task definitions.
- **Kill conditions before paid trials:** A brute-force 'call every tool in every server, then synthesize with a strong LLM' baseline reaches full correctness and full claim coverage without a discovery-precision or parallelism penalty strong enough to separate it from genuine discovery. -- Tool names/descriptions alone (ignoring documented schemas) are sufficient for naive keyword matching to find the correct subset -- the fuzziness axis isn't real. -- The harness-owned ledger can be read, inferred, or spoofed by the agent (verbose errors, shared logs, a debug endpoint), undermining the evidence-forging and claim-coverage controls. -- The leaky-facade canary build is not detected by the isolation audit -- i.e. ledger-derived data can reach an agent-visible channel undetected -- which invalidates every claim-coverage and dependency-order result the family produces, since the entire grading signal depends on that boundary holding. -- The generic-diligence-probe baseline, given only the scrubbed public instructions and no family coaching, already clears a graded axis at or near ceiling on a candidate instance -- that axis is not discriminating skill and the instance/axis must be rejected before trial, on pain of repeating CAA V1's null "valid but easy" result. -- Parallelism-awareness scoring is not reproducible across repeated runs of the same reference trajectory, or a correct serial and a correct parallel trajectory cannot be reliably distinguished programmatically. -- The reference/positive-work solver fails to reach full marks on 5/5 reproducibility runs, or needs per-instance hand-tuning rather than following one general discovery-and-threading policy. -- Backing per-server datasets are static/small enough that a memorized generic answer passes multiple distinct scenario instances. -- The constructed catalog or scenarios turn out to reuse or closely paraphrase foundry-mcp-bench's actual public tool or task definitions rather than being original fictional designs.
- **Adversarial verification:** 3/3 skeptics refuted; card was flagged and strengthened.

### Multi-Day State Carryforward Drift (`multi-day-state-carryforward-drift`)

- **Base family definition:** Unchanged, restated for reference: "A single trajectory simulates extended, multi-day professional work where later steps depend on state correctly carried forward from many earlier steps; a deterministic verifier inspects only the final end-state, so any single dropped or drifted piece of upstream context propagates silently to failure." Task shape: a long chain of dependent operations over simulated extended time in a rich, multi-component environment; the agent plans and executes without intermediate checkpoint feedback, and some steps require genuine cross-domain expert judgment. Causal axis: long-horizon-planning-execution-monitoring. Domain: cross-domain terminal professional work (office/scientific/engineering/sysadmin). Authority boundary: the agent's authority is scoped to the full multi-step task; the verifier is binary against the fully carried-forward final state with no partial credit for locally-correct-looking intermediate steps. Subject-inaccessible witness: no intermediate checkpoint signal is available during execution; whether earlier state was correctly carried forward is only knowable at the final verifier. Natural scenario dimensions: number of dependent stages simulated; richness of the multi-component environment; required domain expertise; execution time budget. Why agents may fail: reported gaps attribute failure to long-horizon planning, execution monitoring, and error recovery, with a documented ~27-point score jump from extending the time budget alone. Why fair: full scope and rules are communicated up front; difficulty comes from sustaining correct execution over many steps, not hidden information. None of this is altered by the construction recipe below -- the recipe only supplies HOW to build, verify, and validate an instance of this already-defined family.

RECONCILIATION NOTE (post three-skeptic review, all three refuted): all three skeptics independently converged on the same two concrete, checkable gaps -- (1) the self-check-coverage "predictor" was a runtime, agent-writable annotation count that the private verifier also consulted for diagnostics, which is the exact self-declared-evidence pattern the verifier architecture claims never to trust, and is gameable by padding annotations; (2) the tool-mediated system-of-record's non-leakage/tamper-resistance was asserted, never proven with a negative control, unlike this repo's own Phase 17 C8-witness-isolation-with-canary precedent. Two skeptics additionally flagged reference/mechanism thinness (repeated single-field substitution; no numeric floor on graded-fact count) and the public brief stating the grading mechanics (supersession + no-intermediate-feedback) nearly as directly as CAA1 named its anti-pattern. All four are fixed by construction below, not argued away: the predictor is rebuilt as a construction-time-only, non-agent-touching archetype battery (see privateVerifierArchitecture, scenarioSelectionStrategy); a store-forgery mutant and a leaky-variant canary are added (see narrowDevMutants #7-8) with matching killConditions; a numeric minimum-fact floor plus a mandatory multi-fact synthesis requirement replace single-field substitution as the graded mechanism (see familyNativeDifficultyOperators, threeExampleInstances); and the public outcome text is rewritten to state only ordinary domain accuracy expectations, with the meta grading-mechanics sentence moved to infoIntentionallyAbsent.
- **Durable-outbox operator mapping:** Mapping of the 10-point recipe onto this family (point 3 is reframed rather than applied literally -- see nonApplicableOperators). Revisions from the post-review hardening pass are marked [HARDENED].

1. Realistic multi-file environment -> a realistic multi-subsystem workplace environment the agent operates across simulated days: an inbox/event feed, a set of professional tool-scripts, and a running state record the agent maintains, spanning enough files and subsystems (scheduling, records, comms, finance-or-lab-or-infra system) that no single file holds the whole task.

2. Outcome-based instructions -> [HARDENED] the brief states only the required end-of-horizon deliverables and ordinary domain accuracy/currency expectations, phrased exactly as a real professional assignment brief would phrase them (see publicOutcomeRequirements). It no longer states the meta grading rule ("later information supersedes earlier information," "you receive no intermediate feedback; only end state is evaluated") -- that sentence was flagged by two independent skeptics as functionally equivalent to CAA1's outright statement of its own tested mechanism. It is removed from public text and moved to infoIntentionallyAbsent: the agent must discover it operationally from the environment's day-gating and the absence of any response to submissions, exactly as a real employee discovers it, not from a labeled rule.

3. Plausible defect naturally embedded -> reframed (see nonApplicableOperators): ordinary plot-native corrections and updates delivered at specific simulated days through normal in-fiction channels, never flagged as wrong, exactly the "stale-state" mechanism already in this repo's mechanism ledger rather than a software defect.

4. Visible tests with a coverage gap -> a day-end self-check tool that verifies per-day submission completeness and schema validity only, never cross-day consistency. [HARDENED] It now exposes no diagnostic signal beyond bare completeness/schema pass-fail -- the earlier draft's "provenance annotation count" is removed entirely (see visibleTestStrategy) because it was agent-writable and therefore gameable/self-declared.

5. External authoritative state / append-only witness -> [HARDENED] a day-gated event feed plus a tool-mediated system-of-record store that the agent can only extend through defined professional actions, enforced as a hard sandbox boundary (read-only mount / separate process, not convention) rather than an asserted convention -- see externalAuthorityOrWitness. This isolation claim now ships with a negative control (narrowDevMutants #8): a deliberately-writable canary build that the same detector must be shown to catch, per this repo's own Phase 17 postmortem recommendation #5.

6. Separate verifier over external outcomes -> an isolated verifier reads only the system-of-record store and the agent's produced deliverable artifacts, never the agent's own running notes, chat narration, self-declared status, or any field of its own state/ files, with no exception (see privateVerifierArchitecture).

7. Positive-work / no-op / always-refuse / unconditional-success / hardcoding / evidence-forging / [HARDENED] store-tamper / isolation-canary / substitution-is-insufficient controls -> see positiveWorkAndCheatControls and narrowDevMutants.

8. Deterministic scenario fuzzing -> fuzz stage count, event placement day, fan-out, environment richness, time budget, and [HARDENED] synthesis-density and overlap-count (see fuzzerDimensions) to find the region where a plausible-but-incomplete agent fails specifically on carryforward and nothing else.

9. Preregistered narrow mutants + held-out validation -> [HARDENED] now eight dev mutants (added store-forgery/tool-bypass and a construction-time-only leaky-variant canary) rather than six -- see narrowDevMutants and heldOutMutants. Additionally, before any instance is selected, a NEW construction-time-only, pre-trial gate runs: a small fixed library of generic, domain-agnostic self-check archetype scripts (completeness-only; same-day-only; final-day full-recency; changed-since-last-touch), zero LLM calls, against the frozen reference trajectory and the frozen event feed, and separately against each narrow dev mutant's final state. This produces a per-instance ranking of which archetype tier is the cheapest one that still catches every narrow mutant without flagging the reference -- directly implementing Phase 17 postmortem recommendation #1 ("add a self-check-coverage predictor to the contract gate, and make it a ranking feature rather than pass/fail"). It never touches any live agent's own submitted artifacts or narration, resolving the exact contradiction three skeptics identified in the prior draft (a verifier that claims never to trust self-declared evidence, then computing a "predictor" from the agent's own annotated state). An instance where a cheap generic archetype already catches every mutant is flagged high-risk-of-being-easy and is redesigned or dropped (killConditions #10) -- the paper-equivalent of the gate that ranked CAA 85 out of 85 before a dollar was spent.

10. Frozen hashes, isolated execution, durable capture, blind labelling -> the event-feed script and system-of-record schema are frozen and hashed before any trial; each attempt runs in its own isolated environment instance; full transcripts, final artifact state, and the system-of-record diff are captured as durable evidence; failing runs are labelled blind to condition. [HARDENED] Every isolation/non-leakage claim in this card now additionally ships with a negative control before it counts as evidence (narrowDevMutants #8, killConditions #9), per Phase 17 postmortem recommendation #5 -- the exact repair for the two Phase 16 CAA controls (witnessIsolated, challengeNonleakage) that had no reachable failing branch.

Mapping of the 8 measured local operators onto this family (as native analogs, not literal reuse), with hardening notes:
- verifier-process-and-ledger-isolation -> the system-of-record store is a separate process/service the agent's submitted artifacts have no write access to except through audited tool calls; [HARDENED] this claim now ships with a negative control (narrowDevMutants #8): a deliberately-writable canary build the same boundary must be shown to catch, not merely asserted to hold.
- a2-explicit-terminal-state-repair -> the visible spec explicitly states which submitted artifacts are final-of-record per stage, without naming any historical anti-pattern.
- narrow-mutant-isolation -> each dev mutant drops or corrupts exactly one carried-forward fact category (or, for #7, one write-path integrity property), holding the rest of the environment fixed, so a failure attributes to one mechanism.
- b6-rig-integrity-controls -> every scenario rig must show reference passes clean, each targeted mutant fails only its own checks, and malformed/absent submissions are refused; [HARDENED] extended with a store-forgery/tool-bypass mutant (narrowDevMutants #7) so the WRITE boundary, not only the READ (day-gating) boundary, is exercised.
- committed-idempotency-authority -> native analog "persisted-record-over-recompute": the correct approach commits each day's authoritative fact into the agent's own running state when learned and reads that persisted record later. [HARDENED] At least two graded facts per instance must additionally be DERIVED values requiring synthesis across multiple independently-arriving upstream facts under an explicit domain reconciliation policy (proration, netting, or compound multi-field consistency for an overlap entity) -- a single field substituted for another is insufficient by construction, closing the "repeated simple substitution" gap two skeptics identified.
- narrow-recompute-starter -> if any starter scaffold is provided, it may show the tempting "always look up the current value" pattern on one non-graded example category only, never on the graded one.
- fuzz-controlling-parameter -> fuzz (stage count, event placement, fan-out, richness, time budget, synthesis-density, overlap-count) to locate the activation region.
- recompute-activation-concentration -> [HARDENED] concretely floored at >=4 independently-verifiable graded facts per instance, >=2 requiring synthesis, and >=1 entity touched by 2+ overlapping drift categories, enforced as killConditions #7 (previously an implicit/aspirational goal with no numeric floor).
- **Non-applicable operators:** - Recipe point 3 as literally stated (a defect embedded in existing code that the agent must locate and repair) does not apply: there is no codebase with a bug to find. It is reframed instead as a naturally superseding fact (see outboxOperatorMapping #3).
- Crash-injection, lease/epoch fencing tokens, and duplicate-side-effect/exactly-once tool-retry semantics do not apply verbatim: there is no single external side-effecting call with an ambiguous UNKNOWN completion status, and no natural process-crash surface. Random crashes or lease revocations would be unnatural here and are explicitly excluded.
- A literal append-only "outbox" ledger structure is not the family's natural witness; the natural external authority is a system-of-record store fed by day-gated events -- functionally analogous (subject-inaccessible, tool-mediated, verifier-read) but not an outbox.
- "duplicate-side-effects" style dedup concerns are not central: this family is about information fidelity across simulated time, not avoiding a repeated external effect under retry.
- Idempotency-key recovery across "lease epochs" specifically does not transfer; the native analog kept is only the shape "persist now, do not recompute later," not the mechanics of keys, leases, or epochs.
- [HARDENED, clarifying] The newly-added store-forgery dev mutant (narrowDevMutants #7) and leaky-variant canary (#8) test only whether the tool-mediated WRITE boundary is a hard sandbox boundary; they are not a reintroduction of crash/lease/exactly-once semantics, which remain excluded for the reasons above. They exist because two skeptics correctly noted that no mutant previously tested writing/forging the record directly (only reading ahead was tested), not because the outbox's retry mechanics have become relevant.
- **Family-native difficulty operators:** - persisted-record-over-recompute (difficulty): correct execution requires committing each day's authoritative fact into a running state artifact when learned, then reading that persisted record downstream; an agent that instead recomputes from final-day evidence gets it wrong because final-day evidence is deliberately insufficient.
- multi-fact synthesis over field substitution (difficulty, construction requirement) [HARDENED, new]: at least two graded facts per instance must be DERIVED values combining two or more independently-arriving upstream facts under an explicit domain reconciliation policy (proration, netting, or compound multi-field consistency for an entity touched by overlapping events). A plain "copy the latest value into the field" substitution is insufficient by construction and is the excluded thin-reference shape (killConditions #7) that review identified as structurally identical to CAA's "one rule applied repeatedly."
- tamper-resistant write boundary (validity-control, family-native) [HARDENED, new]: the tool-mediated write path into the system-of-record must be a hard sandbox boundary, proven with a store-forgery mutant and a deliberately-leaky canary variant (narrowDevMutants #7-8), not merely asserted -- an unproven write-boundary claim is the same unfalsifiable-isolation pattern this repo's Phase 16 CAA controls already exhibited once.
- simulated-time information gating / non-re-derivability (validity-control, family-native): at least one graded fact must be genuinely unrecoverable from what remains accessible at the end of the horizon, tested by the last-day-cram mutant.
- silent narrow propagation (the causal claim itself): a single dropped or drifted upstream fact must propagate to exactly the final deliverables that legitimately depend on it and no others.
- time-budget sensitivity (scenario-selection): time budget must be a first-class fuzzed dimension, not held constant by accident.
- **Multi-file codebase sketch:** A single environment root per scenario instance, e.g.:

- inbox/day1/ ... inbox/dayN/ -- memos, emails, corrected data files, and policy updates; each day's directory is unreadable (or absent) until the agent advances the simulated clock past that day. Frozen and hashed at authoring time.
- state/ -- the agent's own running record, its authoritative memory of what it has learned and decided; this is where "persisted-record-over-recompute" either happens or doesn't. [HARDENED] No annotation convention is mandated or expected here anymore -- the earlier draft's "provenance annotation" idea is removed; whatever the agent writes here is never read by the verifier or by any coverage predictor.
- tools/ -- CLI wrappers standing in for real professional systems. [HARDENED] Mounted read-only to the agent (or run as a separate process the agent has no filesystem access to); calling a tool is the only way to write to the external system-of-record, and each successful call produces a hashed receipt logged append-only outside the agent's writable sandbox.
- self-check/day-end-checklist -- validates per-day submission completeness and schema shape only; exposes no other signal.
- outbound/ -- the agent's final deliverables, also read by the private verifier alongside the system-of-record.
- (private, not in the agent's tree) system-of-record/ -- the authoritative external store, populated independently by the harness, mutated only through tools/ calls with a matching receipt, read only by the isolated post-hoc verifier.
- (construction-time only, never shipped to the agent or the runtime verifier) dev/self-check-archetypes/ -- the fixed library of generic, domain-agnostic archetype scripts (completeness-only, same-day-only, final-day full-recency, changed-since-last-touch) used once, at authoring time, to compute the self-check-coverage predictor against the frozen reference and dev mutants. [HARDENED, new]
- (construction-time only) a leaky-variant canary build -- the same instance with tools/ made agent-writable or the store directly mounted read-write, used once to prove the isolation/tamper detector catches a real leak, then discarded. [HARDENED, new]

Enough real subsystems that no single file or command holds the whole task.
- **Public outcome requirements:** [HARDENED] Stated only as ordinary professional accuracy/currency expectations, in the same words a real assignment brief would use -- never as a description of the test mechanism, never naming "drift," "carryforward," "supersession," or the fact that intermediate days carry no feedback. General template: "By the end of day N, every deliverable in outbound/ must be submitted through the provided tools, schema-valid, and accurate as of the moment you submit it, for each item you are responsible for." Each instance then states its own ordinary domain-accuracy clause in place of a meta rule, e.g. (procurement): "A submitted PO reconciliation must reflect the vendor's current price, the PO's current cost-center allocation, and the quantity actually received." This is table-stakes professional language -- accuracy and currency are assumed of any real submission -- not a hint about what is checked, how many facts are checked, or which day is load-bearing. The previous draft's explicit sentence ("information you receive on a given day supersedes anything you were told earlier... you will not receive feedback on intermediate days; only the end state is evaluated") is removed from public text: two independent skeptics flagged it as functionally equivalent to CAA1 stating its own tested mechanism outright. That content still describes a true property of the harness, but it is now discovered operationally (through day-gating and the absence of any response to submissions) rather than announced -- see infoIntentionallyAbsent.
- **Information intentionally absent from public instruction:** - Which specific fact(s) are actually checked at the end (the graded subset is a proper subset of everything touched).
- Which day's event is "load-bearing" for which deliverable (the fan-out structure).
- That a private system-of-record exists, its schema, or that it is the source of truth read by the verifier.
- That the day-end checklist has a known coverage gap, or what that gap is.
- The narrow-mutant taxonomy or the existence of held-out validation scenarios.
- Per-fact scoring weights or partial-credit rules (there are none).
- That recomputing everything from final-day evidence alone is insufficient by design.
- [HARDENED, new] That intermediate days carry no feedback signal and only final state is evaluated -- a property of the harness the agent may infer from the absence of any response to its actions, never stated in the brief.
- [HARDENED, new] That the recency/supersession logic is "the rule being tested" -- the brief states only ordinary domain accuracy/currency expectations in the same words a real assignment would use, never a labeled meta-rule.
- [HARDENED, new] That a construction-time self-check-coverage predictor, an isolation canary, or a store-forgery mutant were used to build or validate this instance.
- [HARDENED, new] Which graded facts require synthesis versus substitution, or that any distinction between the two exists.
- **Visible-test strategy:** A day-end self-check tool the agent may run any number of times: it validates that each day's required submissions exist, are well-formed, and were made through the correct tool. It deliberately does not check cross-day numerical or logical consistency against superseded values. [HARDENED] It exposes no other diagnostic signal -- no annotation count, no coverage score, nothing beyond bare completeness/schema pass-fail. The prior draft's "provenance annotation count, surfaced as a self-check-coverage predictor" is removed entirely: it was computed from the agent's own writable state fields, required an unmandated annotation convention a fully-correct agent might never use, and was consulted by the private verifier for diagnostics -- three independent skeptics identified this as the same self-declared, gameable evidence the verifier architecture claims never to trust. This narrow scope mirrors a realistic professional day-end checklist (did you file it) rather than a full audit; the gap is source-supported by the family's own claim that no intermediate checkpoint signal exists for carryforward correctness. A genuine self-check-coverage predictor does exist for this family, but it now lives entirely at construction time, outside the agent's environment and outside the runtime verifier -- see scenarioSelectionStrategy and privateVerifierArchitecture.
- **Private verifier architecture:** An isolated process, run only after the agent's session ends, that:
- reads the system-of-record store (populated by the harness independently of the agent, mutated only through the agent's audited, receipted tool calls) and the agent's final outbound/ deliverables;
- never reads the agent's running notes, chat transcript narration, self-declared status, or any field of the agent's own state/ files as evidence of anything, with no exception -- this repo's own Phase 17 postmortem (section 9: "a self-check is not marked green from model prose") is applied here without carve-out, closing the exact contradiction three skeptics identified in the prior draft;
- computes the "should be true as of the final day" value for each graded fact independently, at scenario-authoring time, from the frozen event-feed script -- never by re-executing or trusting the agent's own derivation; for synthesis-requiring facts it computes the full derived value (the prorated/netted/compound result), so partial credit for "got the inputs right but not the combination" is structurally impossible;
- checks per-fact agreement and returns an overall binary pass/fail with no partial credit;
- [HARDENED, new] additionally runs a store-forgery check: any system-of-record entry lacking a matching, hashed tool-call receipt is an automatic fail regardless of whether its values happen to be correct;
- shares no code path or process with the agent's environment, so a submitted deliverable cannot rewrite or read back its own grade.

[HARDENED] Separately, and entirely outside this runtime verifier's code path, a construction-time self-check-coverage predictor is computed once per instance, before any trial exists, from three frozen, non-agent sources only: the reference trajectory, the frozen event feed, and a small fixed library of hand-authored generic archetype scripts (completeness-only; same-day-only; final-day full-recency; changed-since-last-touch -- see scenarioSelectionStrategy). It is run against the reference (must show zero flags) and against each narrow dev mutant's final state (must show the mutant is only caught by the expensive changed-since-last-touch archetype, never by a cheap one). This predictor never reads any live agent's submitted artifacts or narration, and is never itself an input to any attempt's grade -- the earlier draft blurred a runtime, agent-writable diagnostic with this predictor's role, which is exactly the self-declared-evidence problem this section forbids. They are now fully separated: the runtime verifier reads only harness-populated, receipted state; the predictor reads only author-frozen, pre-trial artifacts; neither reads the other's inputs.
- **External authority / witness:** Two paired, subject-inaccessible authorities:
1. The day-gated event feed -- authored and hashed before the run, released only as the agent advances the simulated clock. The agent cannot read ahead or retroactively edit an earlier day.
2. The tool-mediated system-of-record store -- the authoritative record of final state, which the agent can only extend through defined professional tool calls. [HARDENED] This is now an enforced hard sandbox boundary, not an asserted convention: tools/ is mounted read-only to the agent (or run as a separate process it cannot write to), and every successful tool call produces a hashed receipt logged append-only outside the agent's writable sandbox; the private verifier treats any store entry lacking a matching receipt as an automatic fail. This claim ships with two negative controls that did not exist in the prior draft: a store-forgery mutant (narrowDevMutants #7) that attempts to write the store or fabricate a receipt directly, which must be blocked or detected; and a deliberately-leaky canary build (narrowDevMutants #8) that must be caught by the same detector, proving the detector can catch a real leak rather than merely never firing -- the specific repair this repo's own Phase 17 postmortem (recommendation #5) prescribes for exactly the two Phase 16 CAA controls (witnessIsolated, challengeNonleakage) that had no reachable failing branch.
- **Fuzzer dimensions:** - number of dependent stages/simulated days
- number of independent drift-event categories in flight concurrently (fan-out and interleaving)
- which day each event is introduced
- how many downstream deliverables a single event legitimately touches
- environment richness (number of subsystems/files/tools in play)
- execution/time budget
- presence of distractor events that must explicitly NOT be carried forward
- [HARDENED, new] synthesis-density: the proportion of graded facts that are derived (proration/netting/compound multi-field consistency) versus simple substitution
- [HARDENED, new] overlap-count: the number of entities touched by 2+ independent drift-event categories simultaneously
- **Narrow development mutants:** 1. day-1-freeze -- correctly handles day 1, then never incorporates any later event. Predicted failure: every drift-dependent check, and only those.
2. single-drop -- incorporates every event correctly except one specific category held constant across the run. Predicted failure: only the checks depending on that one category, including any synthesis-requiring check whose inputs include the dropped category (proving the synthesis logic is actually exercised, not bypassed).
3. last-day-cram (validity probe) -- makes no per-day updates and attempts to reconstruct the full history from whatever remains accessible on the final day. Must fail; if it passes, that instance's core claim is false.
4. narration-only (evidence-forging control) -- writes a running status log claiming completion without ever calling the real submission/record tools. Must fail everything.
5. always-refuse (no-op control) -- takes no actions at all. Must fail every completion check.
6. hardcoded-final-answer (hardcoding control, construction-time only) -- given illegitimate advance access to later-day files, attempts to write final values on day 1. Must be blocked by day-gated READ access; proves the read boundary only.
7. [HARDENED, new] store-forgery / tool-bypass (integrity control) -- writes directly to the system-of-record's storage path, or fabricates a tool-call audit-log entry, without invoking the real tool wrapper. Must be blocked by sandbox write permissions or detected by the verifier's receipt check (an entry with no matching hashed receipt is an automatic fail regardless of whether its values happen to be correct). Proves the WRITE boundary, distinct from mutant 6's read-boundary test -- added because no mutant in the prior draft tested writing or forging the record directly, a gap three skeptics flagged.
8. [HARDENED, new] leaky-variant canary (construction-time-only negative control, not a real threat model) -- a deliberately misconfigured build of the same instance where tools/ is left agent-writable or the system-of-record path is mounted read-write into the agent's sandbox. Must be caught (flagged or failed) by the same isolation/tamper check used on the locked-down build; if it passes undetected, the real build's non-leakage claim is unfalsifiable as constructed and the instance must not ship (killConditions #9). Mirrors this repo's own C8-witness-isolation-with-canary precedent and closes the exact "no reachable failing branch" defect Phase 17 found in two Phase 16 CAA controls.
- **Held-out mutants:** A fresh domain instance not used to tune the fuzz grid or calibrate activation (e.g., an editorial/publishing production schedule), run against the frozen scenario-selection rules chosen on the calibration domains, plus fresh single-drop mutants targeting event categories never used during dev-mutant tuning. [HARDENED] Additionally: a held-out store-forgery/tool-bypass analog (a different tamper vector on the held-out domain, e.g. overwriting the tool wrapper's own source rather than the store directly) and a held-out leaky-variant canary, both run against the frozen isolation/tamper check. All of the following must reproduce on the held-out domain and held-out mutants before the recipe is treated as validated rather than overfit: reference clean; narrow mutants isolated to their own checks, including synthesis-requiring checks; last-day-cram still failing; the held-out store-forgery analog still blocked/detected; the held-out canary still caught; and the construction-time self-check-coverage predictor still requiring the expensive changed-since-last-touch archetype rather than a cheap one.
- **Positive-work / cheat controls:** - Positive-work control: a fully correct trace doing genuine complete work across every day, including every synthesis computation, must pass all checks with no partial credit needed.
- No-op control: always-refuse (mutant 5) must fail every completion check.
- Unconditional-success control: a mutant submitting plausible, schema-valid but unverified/placeholder deliverables without reading any inbox event must fail.
- [HARDENED, new] Substitution-is-insufficient control: a mutant that copies the single latest value into every graded field without performing the required proration/netting/compound-consistency synthesis must fail the synthesis-requiring checks specifically, proving those checks cannot be satisfied by simple substitution alone.
- Hardcoding control: mutant 6, gated by day-locked READ access.
- [HARDENED, new] Store-tamper / tool-bypass control: mutant 7 must be blocked at the sandbox layer or detected by the verifier's receipt check, proving the WRITE boundary is enforced, not only the day-gated read boundary.
- [HARDENED, new] Isolation canary control: mutant 8, a deliberately-leaky variant, must be caught by the same isolation/tamper check used on the real build -- the negative control this repo's own Phase 17 postmortem (recommendation #5) requires for every isolation/non-leakage claim.
- Evidence-forging control: mutant 4, defeated by the verifier reading only the system-of-record and outbound/ deliverables, never narration.
- Rig-integrity (B6-style) control: reference passes clean, each targeted mutant fails exactly and only its predicted checks, malformed/absent submissions are refused/labelled distinctly.
- At least one day per scenario where the professionally correct action is genuinely "no update needed," so always-refuse's failure is attributable to missing required action, not an artifact of scenario design.
- **Scenario-selection strategy:** Run the fuzzer grid (see fuzzerDimensions) with the dev-mutant bank plus the reference across candidate combinations. Select the region where: the reference passes 100% of authored instances; each narrow mutant fails specifically and only its predicted checks; last-day-cram fails; the store-forgery mutant (#7) is blocked or detected; the leaky-variant canary (#8) is caught by the isolation/tamper check; and no region requires stacking more independently-motivated drift events than the minimum-fact floor already requires.

[HARDENED] Additionally, before an instance is selected, it must clear two construction-time gates absent from the prior draft:

(a) Minimum-fact floor: at least 4 independently-verifiable graded facts, at least 2 requiring synthesis of multiple independently-arriving upstream facts under an explicit domain reconciliation policy (not single-field substitution), and at least one entity touched by 2+ overlapping drift-event categories. An instance below this floor is the excluded thin-reference shape (killConditions #7) and is redesigned or dropped.

(b) Self-check-coverage predictor: run the fixed library of generic, domain-agnostic self-check archetype scripts (completeness-only; same-day-only; final-day full-recency; changed-since-last-touch), zero LLM calls, against the frozen reference and each narrow dev mutant's final state. An instance where a cheap archetype (completeness-only or same-day-only) already catches every narrow mutant is flagged high-risk-of-being-easy -- the paper-equivalent of CAA's 85-out-of-85 ranking -- and is redesigned (more volume/fan-out/overlap to make the cheap archetype infeasible within the fuzzed time budget) or dropped, per killConditions #10.

Reject regions of under-activation and over-activation via artificial complexity. Keep the authored instance count small and materially distinct rather than combinatorial. Confirm on held-out mutants (including the held-out store-forgery analog and canary) and a held-out domain before treating the construction as validated.
- **Cheapest package-validity probe:** Before any paid agent trial, entirely scripted, no LLM calls, well under an hour: run the reference on every authored instance (must be all-pass, including every synthesis-requiring derived field, not just substituted fields); run day-1-freeze and single-drop mutants (must fail exactly their predicted checks); run always-refuse and narration-only mutants (must fail everything); run last-day-cram (must fail); attempt to read a not-yet-unlocked inbox/dayN+1 file directly (must be blocked). [HARDENED, new steps:] run the store-forgery mutant -- write directly to the system-of-record path or fabricate a tool-call log entry without calling the real tool -- (must be blocked at the sandbox permission layer or detected by the verifier's receipt check); build and run the deliberately-leaky canary variant through the same isolation/tamper check used on the locked-down build (must be flagged/caught -- if it passes undetected, the instance does not ship, per killConditions #9); and run the construction-time self-check-coverage predictor archetype battery against the reference and every narrow dev mutant (every mutant must require at least the changed-since-last-touch archetype to be caught, not a cheap completeness/same-day-only one -- if a cheap archetype already catches everything, the instance does not ship, per killConditions #10). All of this is a clean go/no-go gate before spending trial budget.
- **Three example instances:**
  1. Procurement/finance (5 simulated days; floor satisfied: 4 independently-verifiable graded facts, 2 requiring synthesis, 1 entity affected by 3 overlapping drift categories): the agent manages purchase orders #1001-#1006. Day 1: initial vendor quotes and provisional unit prices arrive; the agent logs POs and provisional totals. Day 2: one vendor issues a corrected unit price and a partial-backorder notice (reduced shipped quantity) for two line items, including PO #1004. Day 3: finance reassigns PO #1004 across two cost centers on a 60/40 split following a reorg memo -- a proration, not a full reassignment. Day 4: a shipment arrives with a further quantity discrepancy on PO #1004, which must be netted against the Day-2 backorder figure, not treated as a fresh independent number. Day 5: the agent submits final reconciliations through the provided finance tool for all six POs. Outcome requirement, in ordinary business terms only: 'Submitted reconciliations must show, for each PO, the vendor's current unit price, the PO's current cost-center allocation, and the net quantity actually received; PO #1004's reconciliation must show the prorated 60/40 split applied to the netted quantity at the corrected price -- a single recomputed line, not three separately-patched fields.' Graded facts: corrected price (substitution); reassigned cost-center for the five non-split POs (substitution); PO #1004's netted quantity (synthesis: Day-2 backorder + Day-4 discrepancy); PO #1004's prorated-split final total (synthesis: price x netted quantity x 60/40 allocation, combining three upstream facts). External witness: a finance system-of-record the agent can only write to via the reconciliation tool, mounted read-only everywhere else; a deliberately-writable canary build and a store-forgery mutant (writing the ledger directly) must both be handled correctly before this instance ships.
  1. Lab operations (6 simulated days; same floor: 4 graded facts, 2 requiring synthesis): the agent runs a multi-sample analytical chemistry workflow. Day 1: reagent lots are logged with lot-specific concentrations; sample IDs assigned, including S-14. Day 2: an instrument recalibration memo changes the conversion factor applied to raw sensor readings going forward. Day 3: a correction memo reassigns two mislabeled sample IDs, one of which is S-14. Day 4: an environmental log flags a temperature excursion covering a specific two-hour window that invalidates only readings taken in that window, not the full day's batch; S-14's reading falls inside that window. Days 5-6: the agent compiles the final analysis report. Outcome requirement, in ordinary lab-reporting terms: 'The final report must state each sample's corrected ID and its concentration computed with the currently-calibrated conversion factor; any reading taken during a flagged excursion window must be reported as invalidated, and only readings actually inside that window may be excluded.' Graded facts: corrected sample IDs (substitution); the recalibrated conversion factor applied to every post-Day-2 raw value (synthesis: raw value x new factor, not a copied constant); the exact excursion-window subset excluded while same-day non-window readings are kept (synthesis: a targeted time-window judgment, not a blanket day-level flag); S-14's report line, which must show the corrected ID, recalibrated concentration, and invalidation flag simultaneously (the overlap entity). External witness: a LIMS-style record store with the same read-only mount, forgery-mutant, and canary requirements as the other instances.
  1. Infrastructure migration (4 simulated days; same floor): the agent migrates a small server fleet. Day 1: hosts migrate to new IP ranges and an IP mapping is recorded, including host db-03. Day 2: an access-control change revokes one team's SSH key and issues a replacement fingerprint. Day 3: a naming-convention update renames a subset of hostnames, including renaming db-03 to db-eastus-03 -- db-03 is now affected by both the Day-1 IP change and the Day-3 rename simultaneously. Day 4: the agent finalizes DNS records, firewall rules, and a CMDB entry. Outcome requirement, in ordinary ops terms: 'Final DNS, firewall, and CMDB entries must use each host's current name, current IP, and the current (non-revoked) key fingerprint; a firewall rule for a host whose name and IP both changed must reference both the new name and the new IP together in the same rule, not the old name with only the IP patched or vice versa.' Graded facts: final IP mapping (substitution); current key fingerprint (substitution); renamed hostnames (substitution); db-03/db-eastus-03's firewall rule specifically, requiring the renamed hostname and migrated IP to agree in one rule (synthesis: a compound two-field update on one overlap entity, not two independent patches). External witness: a CMDB/DNS system-of-record modifiable only via the provided apply tool, never by editing zone files directly, with the same read-only mount, forgery-mutant, and canary requirements as the other instances.
- **Estimated ordinary-to-recipe uplift:** Framed as ordinary-version-to-recipe-hardened-version, not additive per-operator credit: an "ordinary" build of this family (single long instruction list, agent-writable state files trusted as ground truth, no enforced day-gating, no narrow-mutant validation, graded facts satisfiable by copying the latest value) would let a submission fabricate a correct-looking final state cheaply. This hardened build closes more cheap wins than the prior draft: (i) tool-mediated system-of-record instead of agent-writable "truth," now with a proven (canaried) tamper-resistant write boundary rather than an asserted one; (ii) day-gating validated by the last-day-cram probe; (iii) [HARDENED, new] an agent can no longer reach a correct-looking final state via single-field substitution alone, since at least two graded facts per instance require genuine multi-fact synthesis under a stated domain policy; (iv) [HARDENED, new] a construction-time predictor now screens out instances a cheap, generic self-check would solve by accident, rather than discovering that fact only after a paid campaign, as happened with the CAA candidate. This remains a hypothesis about which shortcuts get closed, not a measured solve-rate delta; per this repo's own discipline, no numeric uplift is reported until a paired trial with reward-zero attempts under the hardened build actually runs.
- **Main fairness/validity risks:** - Imperfect day-gating could let an agent infer structure unfairly, or over-block information that should legitimately already be available -- gating must match the family's own day-boundary concept exactly.
- If a load-bearing fact requires implausible or narrow specialist expertise to recognize as relevant, failure attributes to missing domain knowledge rather than long-horizon planning/monitoring. [HARDENED] This now extends explicitly to the new synthesis requirement: any proration/netting/compound-consistency rule must be resolvable by ordinary domain professional judgment applied to the facts actually delivered, not by specialized expertise beyond the stated role -- if a synthesis rule requires unstated specialist knowledge to even recognize as necessary, the instance must be dropped or the missing rule stated as an ordinary domain norm.
- The verifier must never become a scoring input based on timing or intermediate path -- only final state is graded, with no credit or penalty for how locally correct intermediate steps looked. [HARDENED] This risk is now structurally closed rather than merely stated: the self-check-coverage predictor is construction-time-only and never touches a live attempt's artifacts, so it cannot become a runtime scoring input even by accident.
- Risk of rewarding a "keep everything provisional, reconcile only at the end" strategy if some graded facts turn out fully recoverable at the end anyway -- mitigated by the last-day-cram probe; any instance where that probe passes must be fixed or dropped.
- The visible day-end checklist's coverage gap must stay genuine and source-supported, not artificially blinded to dodge exactly the graded dimension.
- Time-budget confound: any comparison between conditions or agents must hold time budget fixed or treat it explicitly as fuzzed.
- [HARDENED, new] Removing the meta-supersession/no-intermediate-feedback sentence from the public brief must not cross into withholding a DOMAIN fact a real professional would need -- only the grading-mechanics meta-statement is withheld; the ordinary domain accuracy/currency expectation remains stated in every instance's brief, preserving the family's own whyFair claim that full scope and rules are communicated up front.
- [HARDENED, new] If the tool wrappers or the system-of-record's storage path are not verifiably tamper-proof to the agent (enforced by sandbox permissions, not convention), a subject could fabricate an audited-looking action without doing the underlying work, silently invalidating every downstream result; this is now proven with a negative control (the leaky-variant canary), not merely asserted.
- [HARDENED, new] The construction-time self-check-coverage predictor must never be computed from, or influenced by, any live agent's own submitted artifacts or narration -- it runs once, at authoring time, on frozen non-agent artifacts only; any design that lets a live attempt's own state feed back into a "coverage" signal would reintroduce the self-declared-evidence problem the verifier architecture forbids.
- **Kill conditions before paid trials:** 1. Day-gating cannot be enforced as a hard boundary in the available sandbox/tooling and cannot be fixed -- look-ahead and hardcoding can no longer be ruled out.
2. The last-day-cram mutant passes on an authored instance -- the "no intermediate checkpoint" framing is vacuous for it.
3. Single-drop mutants cannot be tuned to fail only their targeted checks -- individual carried-forward facts cannot be isolated from generic task-execution noise.
4. The positive-work reference cannot reliably reach a clean pass because the required cross-domain judgment call is itself underspecified or genuinely ambiguous to a careful human.
5. No plausible external system-of-record analog exists for a candidate domain instance without bolting on an unnatural literal ledger -- that instance is dropped.
6. Activation only becomes reliable by stacking more than one independently-motivated drift event or adding complexity unrelated to the professional task.
7. [HARDENED, new] Fewer than 4 independently-verifiable graded facts per instance, or fewer than 2 requiring genuine multi-fact synthesis under an explicit domain policy rather than single-field substitution, or no entity touched by 2+ overlapping drift categories -- the excluded thin single-decision/pure-substitution shape two skeptics identified in the prior draft.
8. [HARDENED, new] The tool-mediated write boundary into the system-of-record cannot be enforced as a hard sandbox boundary -- the store-forgery mutant (narrowDevMutants #7) is not reliably blocked or detected -- and cannot be fixed.
9. [HARDENED, new] The deliberately-leaky canary build (narrowDevMutants #8) is not caught by the isolation/tamper check -- the non-leakage claim is unfalsifiable as constructed and the instance must not ship, per this repo's own Phase 16 CAA precedent of exactly this defect.
10. [HARDENED, new] The construction-time self-check-coverage predictor shows a cheap, generic, domain-agnostic archetype (completeness-only or same-day-only) already catches every narrow dev mutant, and the instance cannot be redesigned (more fan-out/volume/overlap) to require the expensive changed-since-last-touch archetype instead -- the paper-equivalent of the CAA candidate scoring 85 out of 85 on this axis; the instance must be redesigned or dropped, not shipped with a caveat.
- **Adversarial verification:** 3/3 skeptics refuted; card was flagged and strengthened.


## 11. Task-capacity analysis

## 11. Task-capacity analysis

**Scope.** This section analyzes the ten-family portfolio submitted for this review round (`webhook-out-of-order-state-overwrite` through `multi-day-state-carryforward-drift`). Every one of the ten recipe cards carries three independent skeptic reviews and a unanimous verdict: **30/30 refuted**, `flaggedByAdversary: true` on all ten. None has a reference implementation built, none has run a single agent trial (`estimatedUplift` on every card is self-labeled "unmeasured hypothesis" / "fresh-external, not a calibration anchor"), and none has cleared its own `cheapestPackageValidityProbe`. That status is load-bearing for every number below: this is a capacity ceiling for a portfolio still at the paper-design stage, not an inventory of shippable tasks.

**Counting rule.** Unchanged from the project's standing definition: a task counts toward 1,000 only with material differences in codebase, workflow, obligations, diagnosis path, or failure interaction. Renaming, reseeding, reordering, re-thresholding, or re-fixturing does not count.

| tier | what it is in this portfolio | counts toward 1,000? |
|---|---|---|
| scenario | one cell in a family's fuzz grid (e.g. one TTL × purge-type × topology combination in the CDN family, or one stage-count × fan-out × time-budget draw in the carryforward-drift family) | no |
| parameterized instance | one of a family's `threeExampleInstances` — same file architecture, same decision surfaces, same verifier/mutant/canary scaffolding, different domain nouns (subscription vs. invoice vs. marketplace webhook reconciliation; equity vs. card-payment vs. market-data rollback fleet) | no |
| challenge package | one hash-frozen bundle — reference + starter + narrow/held-out mutants + verifier + fixtures — for one instance | only if materially distinct from every other package in the portfolio |
| **distinct benchmark task** | different codebase or subsystem set, different embedded defect *and* different diagnosis path, such that fixing one gives no transfer to another | **yes** |

Applied strictly, most of each family's three "example instances" fail this bar on their own terms: the cards themselves describe them as the same file skeleton and the same named decision functions (`dedupe.py`/`apply.py`/`monitor.py`; `purgePlanner.ts`/`convergenceJudge.ts`; `identifier_guard.py`) wearing different domain labels, which is precisely the "changing names/fixtures" case the rule excludes.

### Portfolio capacity

| family | conservative | optimistic | shared infrastructure |
|---|---|---|---|
| Out-of-Order / Split-Event Webhook Reconciliation | 1 | 3 | `platform_sim` truth-log+ledger sidecar, 3-surface `GradedRule` predictor, leaky-facade canary |
| Rollback That Reactivates a Dormant Defect | 1 | 3 | attestation registry + venue ledger, coverage-overclaim canary, 4-decision thickness gate |
| CDN and DNS Cache Staleness Verification | 1 | 2 | split `purgePlanner`/`convergenceJudge` decision, 3-store witness, forgery-cross-check |
| State-Diff Collateral Damage Verification | 1 | 2 | tool-mediated AppWorld-style sandbox, self-audit-affordance predictor, leaky-facade canary |
| Nominally-Successful Batch Hides Partial Failure | 1 | 2 | mock-HIE ledger + hash-chained audit log, 3-baseline self-check predictor |
| SQL/Query Identifier Injection Scope Expansion | 1 | 2 | recorder-proxy witness, raw-driver-bypass canary, stable-id/ancestry-walk mutant pair |
| Verifier Container Isolation Boundary | 1 | 2 | daemon event-stream/inspect-API witness, dual-independent-repair gate, pre-trial predictor |
| Narrow-Assertion Verifier Satisfied Without Real Work | 3 | 5 | root-owned ledger/oracle per instance, half-fix second-decision gate, spec-anchored/generalization coverage split |
| Fuzzy-Instruction Tool Discovery and Dependency Threading | 2 | 5 | trajectory-gateway ledger, generic-diligence-probe contract gate, LLM claim-extraction judge |
| Multi-Day State Carryforward Drift | 2 | 5 | day-gated event feed + tool-mediated system-of-record, archetype-battery predictor, store-forgery canary |
| **portfolio total** | **14** | **31** | one shared construction template (verifier-process-and-ledger isolation, b6 rig-integrity controls, narrow/held-out mutant pairs, a self-check-coverage predictor lineage, a C8-style leaky-facade canary) instantiated 10 times |

Three families reach materially above the portfolio floor because their own cards specify genuinely separate codebases per instance, not just relabeled ones: the narrow-assertion family's order-reservation, billing-exporter, and PII-redaction instances have different domains, different embedded defects, and different witness types (a ledger daemon, a golden differential oracle, and an audit-bucket scan, respectively); the fuzzy-instruction family's three catalogs differ in dependency-graph shape (a width-2 branch, an N-way per-patient fan-out, and a join-then-conditional-dispatch chain); the carryforward-drift family's three domains differ in which synthesis rule is graded (proration, calibration-factor recomputation, compound two-field consistency). The other seven families' three "instances" are, by their own text, the same decision surfaces and the same file skeleton with different domain nouns, so they are held at 1 (conservative) and credited only a packaging allowance, not a task-count allowance, at optimistic.

**Estimated independent causal axes: approximately 5, generously 6** — against 10 nominal families, i.e. roughly 2x reuse per axis:

- **A. Stale/cached signal trusted as authoritative without re-confirming the actual source of record** (premature convergence). Covers the webhook, rollback, CDN/DNS, clinical-bundle, and verifier-container-isolation families — **5 of 10**, exactly half the portfolio.
