The webhook family's freshness/retry-authority surfaces, the CDN family's `convergenceJudge`, the rollback family's attestation-vs-manifest trust, the clinical-bundle family's async/resume convergence, and the container-isolation family's mount-source cache (D1) are the same underlying mechanism wearing five different domain skins.
- **B. Authority/scope-boundary violation** — acting on a broader or different object/field scope than authorized because a plausible narrow-vs-broad affordance pair exists. Covers state-diff collateral damage, SQL/identifier injection scope expansion, and (as a secondary facet) rollback's "fleet-wide remediation without per-replica confirmation" trap — **2-3 of 10**.
- **C. Verifier/checker gameability** — a hollow artifact satisfying a literal or narrow assertion without doing the underlying work. Covers only the narrow-assertion family — **1 of 10**, and arguably a different *kind* of task altogether (it tests the grading methodology, not a domain skill).
- **D. Tool/dependency discovery and orchestration under ambiguity** — covers only the fuzzy-instruction family — **1 of 10** (its "eager-placeholder" sub-mechanism overlaps axis A).
- **E. Long-horizon state persistence and multi-fact synthesis under no intermediate feedback** — covers only the carryforward-drift family — **1 of 10** (its "persisted-record-over-recompute" framing overlaps axis A conceptually).

**Likely shared infrastructure.** All ten cards, independent of domain, are instances of one construction template: a root-owned/process-isolated external witness (ledger, sidecar, daemon-introspection feed, or system-of-record); the same six-control B6 rig (positive-work / no-op / always-refuse / unconditional-success / hardcoding / evidence-forging); a narrow-dev-mutant-plus-held-out-mutant validation split; a self-check-coverage-predictor lineage all tracing to `src/screens/self-check-coverage.ts`; and, in most post-revision cards, a C8-style leaky-facade canary as the negative control for the isolation claim. This gives high leverage — a genuine fix to any one shared component (e.g., an actually-working pre-trial self-check-coverage ranking feature) drops into all ten packages with little marginal engineering — but it is also a single point of systemic failure: all 30 skeptic reviews converged on the **same four defects** in this shared template (reference/mechanism thinness relative to the CAA V1 ~15-line/one-decision bar; public instruction text partially naming the graded anti-pattern; a "self-check-coverage predictor" that is mislabeled, runtime-and-agent-writable, or never actually applied pre-trial; and an isolation/non-leakage claim asserted without a working negative control), not on family-specific domain problems. A defect in the shared template is a defect in the whole portfolio at once.

**Likely duplication rate.** Combining (a) axis-A's 50% concentration across nominal families and (b) each family's own instance-level reskinning (roughly two of every three stated instances are non-material domain relabels of the same decision surfaces), I estimate a **45-55% semantic duplication rate at the optimistic capacities above** — materially worse than a healthy portfolio, and the reason the conservative column, not the optimistic one, is the planning number.

**Maximum recommended portfolio concentration: 10% of any build cohort in any one family, 20% in any one causal axis.** This portfolio as constituted already violates the axis cap by roughly 2x: axis A (stale-witness-trust) supplies 5 of the 10 families. Before any of these proceed to construction spend, at most 2 of the 5 axis-A families should be built in the same cohort — recommend keeping the webhook and clinical-bundle instances (the richest multi-surface designs on that axis) and deferring rollback, CDN/DNS, and verifier-container-isolation until either a distinct axis is substituted for two of them or their construction is demonstrably differentiated beyond "trust a cache, get caught by a fresh re-query."

### The arithmetic that governs the mission

Conservative portfolio capacity is **14 distinct tasks** from these 10 families, contingent on remediating the same four defects every skeptic found — a remediation that has not yet happened for any family. Optimistic capacity, crediting every stated instance the cards claim as independently engineerable, is **31**. Neither number is currently real: zero of these ten families have a built reference, a validated verifier, or a single completed agent trial, so today's actual contribution to the 1,000-task goal is **0**.

Even taking the optimistic ceiling at face value, this portfolio would supply on the order of 1.4-3.1% of a 1,000-task target, and it would do so by exercising only 5-6 independent causal axes — meaning at this portfolio's own construction ratio (≈2 nominal families per axis, ≈3 tasks per family), reaching 1,000 tasks this way would mean testing perhaps 15-25 genuinely independent skills repeated 40-65 times apiece. That is a diversity failure independent of the raw counting-rule arithmetic: scaling this specific recipe-generation pattern to 1,000 tasks is not recommended without deliberately sourcing new causal axes (particularly away from axis A, which is already over-represented), and no family or axis in this cohort should be treated as a template to be cloned toward volume until at least one instance of it survives a real adversarial-construction pass rather than a third consecutive unanimous refutation.

## 12. Four-family experimental cohort

**strongest-existing-evidence** — ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing
- Rationale: This is the only top-20 family whose own definition text is explicitly anchored to the foundry's one measured, replicated local capability gap -- it is built to 'harden the locally shipping UI action record/replay families' and cites the '5/5 trial failure rate' directly. Per instructions, ui-action-record-replay itself is calibration context, not a selectable candidate (it's already SHIP); this family is the correct vehicle for spending build effort against that exact evidence, because its reconciliation independently discovered the mechanism it targets (MutationObserver batching / takeRecords() / disconnect-teardown race) is NOT actually tested by any of the 4 existing artifacts already occupying this niche (ui-action-record-replay, ui-replay-live-dom, the idea-stage browser-action-replay contract shape, and a built-but-unshipped ui-replay-browser-backed Playwright slice) -- so it adds a genuinely untested slice of the same causal axis rather than re-measuring what's already known.
- Existing evidence: reports/ui-action-record-replay-family-report.md: 5/5 counted real-agent trials failed at least one scenario. Verified directly against trials/ui-action-record-replay/*/result.json (33/46/62/62/90 of 324 scenarios failed) and trials/ui-replay-live-dom/*/result.json (219/864 failed, one trial crashed uncounted): 0 of 6 real trials in this exact mechanism lineage achieved a clean sweep. Ship-gate table confirms these two are literally the only 2 of 22 registered families with difficulty-evidenced=pass. A third, previously-uncited artifact (src/families/ui-replay-browser-backed/, reports/ui-replay-browser-backed-report.md) was found to already exist with a validated 12/12-cell mutant-detection slice, giving reusable real-browser infrastructure.
- Must rebuild: The specific batching/takeRecords/teardown-race mechanism itself (net-new fixture + mutants); independent re-verification of the family's own cited primarySourceUnitIds ('boundary-dom-mutation-observer', 'klavis-mcpmark-repo'), which a repo-wide search confirms do not appear anywhere in the discovery corpus or source ledgers and must be re-grounded before the family can pass provenance review.
- Recipe overlay: Reuse the already-built and validated ui-replay-browser-backed Playwright/Chromium harness wholesale rather than standing up new browser infra; reuse ui-action-record-replay/ui-replay-live-dom's scenario-selection and mutant-authoring machinery (selector-drift, stale-handle, aria-busy-honesty mutants) as scaffolding.
- Family-native additions: A minimal fixture page batching 2+ mutations in one microtask, detaching an unrelated subtree with delayed removal notification, and disconnecting a third observer without takeRecords() so pending records are silently dropped; a verifier that checks the agent's replay decision against the true post-flush DOM state, not a premature or dropped-record read.
- Expected build hours: 30
- Cheapest kill gate: Add the one new fixture to the existing ui-replay-browser-backed harness (zero new infra) and run 2-3 frontier agents zero-shot. If they pass cleanly using only reasoning the existing regionFate/anchorConflict/stale-handle mutants already exercise, the family's incremental-gap claim is falsified in an afternoon before any dedicated mutant bank is built.
- Predicted clean 5/6-or-6/6 probability: probabilityOrdinaryPackage 15% (range 8-28%); probabilityFullRecipe 82% (range 68-95%) -- ordinary/naive attempts predicted very hard to pass cleanly (consistent with the 0/6 sibling base rate), while a properly constructed reference/mutant pair is predicted to validate reliably (high full-recipe number reflects construction-quality ceiling, not agent ease).
- What we'd learn even if easy: If frontier agents clear this cleanly even under a hardened build, the foundry's one replicated capability gap (ui-action-record-replay/ui-replay-live-dom) would be shown to be driven by selector-drift/stale-handle mechanics rather than genuine async-DOM-completion reasoning -- narrowing the 'async-out-of-order-completion' axis and showing the existing SHIP signal is mechanism-specific, not a broad async-DOM blind spot.

**caa-lineage-fair-retest** — cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap
- Rationale: This family's own reconciled semanticOverlapGroup explicitly states it 'shares the boundary-cert-transparency-dns source unit directly with the already-built caa-revalidation family' -- the clearest CAA-lineage-adjacent candidate in the top-20. CAA V1 was VALID-BUT-EASY for three diagnosed, fixable reasons: a ~15-20-line single-decision-point reference solution, forbidden-outcomes text that literally named the anti-pattern (letting agents avoid it by recitation rather than reasoning), and no self-check-coverage predictor in the contract gate. This family retests the same 'trust the origin, not a legally-stale delegated copy' reasoning class without inheriting those flaws: it forces a genuine multi-step purge-type/tier-verification-sequence decision (not one boolean), has no one-sentence trap to recite, and its own naturalScenarioDimensions (TTL, purge type, tier topology, rate limits, verification method) give real depth a coverage predictor can grade.
- Existing evidence: No local trial evidence (unbuilt). Calibration context: CAA V1 (Phase 17) scored 4/4 clean solves / VALID-BUT-EASY per reports/PHASE-17-CAA-VALIDATION.md, with causes diagnosed as above. This family's own reconciliation found the 'cached-alias-truster' mutant already exists in deployment-model-alias-rollout-drift, and flags the central trap (one purge + one refetch is not proof of propagation) as well-documented public trivia -- the same easy-recall risk class that sank CAA V1, priced into its probabilityOrdinaryPackage of 45%.
- Must rebuild: A genuine multi-tier cache/DNS simulation (edge+shield tiers, TTL clock, soft/hard/tag/prefix purge endpoints, EXPIRED-vs-MISS semantics, rate limits) -- none of this exists yet; implementationTrialTractability was reconciled low (4), and the two-stage falsifying probe below must run before any of the 36h build is committed, specifically to avoid CAA V1's error of building a full harness before checking the trap survives contact with a frontier model.
- Recipe overlay: Reuse this portfolio's stale-authority/cache-freshness construction pattern already exercised by deployment-model-alias-rollout-drift's cacheFreshness axis and durable-approval-outbox's stale-lease grading discipline (grade via an independent tier-status ledger the subject cannot read, not its own self-report); reuse CAA's own authority-boundary framing (origin is sole truth; delegated copies are time-bounded, not authoritative).
- Family-native additions: A process-level grader scoring the agent's tier-by-tier verification TRAIL (which tier, in what order, EXPIRED vs true MISS distinguished) rather than only end-state content match -- built natively from day one as the direct fix for CAA V1's diagnosed 'no self-check-coverage predictor' gap, instead of retrofitted after a VALID-BUT-EASY postmortem.
- Expected build hours: 36
- Cheapest kill gate: Stage 1, near-zero cost: single-turn, no-tool prompt to 3-5 frontier models describing a concrete purge scenario, no harness access. If models near-uniformly recite the correct multi-tier verification sequence from pretrained knowledge alone, the family is falsified before any environment is built -- the direct fair-retest analog of the probe CAA V1 skipped.
- Predicted clean 5/6-or-6/6 probability: probabilityOrdinaryPackage 45% (range 32-58%); probabilityFullRecipe 30% (range 15-45%, low confidence) -- both well below CAA V1's realized 100% (4/4), which is the intended contrast: genuinely harder if built with the trail-grading fix.
- What we'd learn even if easy: If this still clears cleanly despite trail-based grading and no recitable forbidden-outcomes text, CAA V1's easiness was NOT fully explained by its three diagnosed construction flaws -- the underlying 'trust origin over stale delegated copy' class may simply be reliably solved regardless of construction quality, arguing to retire the whole CAA-adjacent lineage rather than keep funding construction fixes.

**fresh-external-discovery** — narrow-assertion-reward-hacking
- Rationale: evidenceType=fresh-external, sourced from a real Terminal-Bench community discussion and rubric proposal (tb-discussion-224, tb-rubric-proposal) -- genuine fresh external discovery about a documented eval-construction failure mode, not a repackaged existing incident. Its causal axis (verification-strategy-failure) is distinct from the other three selected families. Critically, the supplied Top-10 recipe-card list shows this family already has an existing 'Durable-Outbox Recipe Card (Hardened v2, post-adversarial-reconciliation)' -- construction-recipe work adapting the durable-outbox hardening playbook to this family is already done, substantially lowering real marginal build risk relative to its face-value scores.
- Existing evidence: No local trial evidence (unbuilt). Reconciliation directly verified this repo's B6 known-good/known-bad/malformed-input probe schema (src/phase-15/types.ts) and anti-leak content checks (src/challenge/package-check.ts) are real, applicable, existing tooling; also verified this portfolio's own precedent (reports/candidate-ledger.md, reports/PHASE-15-DISCOVERY-ENGINE.md) that a near-100%/clean-majority pass rate is a pre-registered KILL signal, the calibration lens behind this family's probabilityFullRecipe.
- Must rebuild: A fix for verifier circularity -- no independent ground truth for 'genuine vs hollow' exists beyond a second human-authored checker, so the family needs either real functional/behavioral tests per instance or a documented equivalence class of acceptable alternate implementations; also needs the sandbox configured so the checker file is not trivially visible by default.
- Recipe overlay: Directly reuse the already-existing Durable-Outbox Hardened v2 recipe card for this family: apply its harness-owned-ledger / three-process-split discipline (tool_server owns ground truth; the subject process never imports or can forge the grading state) -- the same fix that closed durable-outbox's three historical verifier bypasses (never-called-tool false pass, monkeypatched-ground-truth false pass, trivially-bypassable role isolation) -- to prevent a subject from reading or shaping its own 'genuine work' grade here.
- Family-native additions: A native loose-arm-vs-strict-arm scenario pair per instance (one deliberately loose/literal checker, one hardened/semantic checker) so the family's core hypothesis -- that construction quality, not raw capability, drives most variance -- is measured directly within each instance.
- Expected build hours: 22
- Cheapest kill gate: Run the existing Hardened v2 recipe's B6 probe triad against one hand-authored loose-checker instance and one strict-checker instance: a scripted hollow-but-literally-passing artifact should clear the loose arm and fail the strict arm, and a genuinely correct but differently-implemented artifact should pass the strict arm. If either fails to discriminate, the reused recipe's checker design needs rework before any agent trial is spent.
- Predicted clean 5/6-or-6/6 probability: probabilityOrdinaryPackage 48% (range 30-65%); probabilityFullRecipe 28% (range 12-45%, low confidence) -- reconciled toward the portfolio-precedent-consistent reading that hardening should suppress, not inflate, the clean-pass rate.
- What we'd learn even if easy: If even the hardened/strict-arm instances clear cleanly, frontier agents reliably produce genuine (non-hollow) deliverables by default given a realistic spec -- meaning narrow-assertion reward hacking is a risk to guard against in this foundry's OWN family-construction process, not itself a discriminating subject-facing capability gap worth funding.

**distinct-causal-axis** — state-diff-collateral-damage-verification
- Rationale: causalAxis=scope-expansion-collateral-damage is cleanly distinct from both excluded axes: it is not a durable-recovery/retry/rollback mechanism (unlike partial-effect-reconciliation-rollback or retries-duplicate-side-effects, which map to the durable-outbox calibration anchor), and it is not an identity/authority-staleness-correspondence mechanism (unlike stale-authority-replay-against-changed-state, used deliberately in the CAA-lineage slot, or authority-boundary-scope-violation). Its subjectInaccessibleWitness -- the agent's own tool-call results cannot reveal collateral damage; only an independent full-world-state diff can -- is one of the cleanest genuinely subject-inaccessible witnesses in the whole top-20 (reconciled subjectInaccessibleAuthority=8), giving it real diagnostic teeth distinct from the other three families' mechanics.
- Existing evidence: No local trial evidence (unbuilt, fresh-external, sourced from foundry-open-discovery / AppWorld-style state-diff methodology). Both independent scorers converged unprompted on the same cheap falsifying-probe design and the same principal validity risk (a raw diff cannot distinguish collateral damage from legitimate intended cascades) -- treated in reconciliation as corroborating, not redundant.
- Must rebuild: The expensive, low-automation part both scorers flagged: a precise, hand-authored per-instance allowlist of intended cascading side effects (linked-record updates, denormalized counters, audit-log writes) so the verifier can distinguish real collateral damage from a correct action's legitimate consequences -- bespoke authoring cost, reconciled constructionOperatorCompatibility only 5.
- Recipe overlay: Adapt access-token-scope-expansion's authority-boundary/blast-radius grading discipline (same semanticOverlapGroup, named independently by both scorers) from a ledger-audited allow/deny decision to a full before/after world-state diff; reuse its grader-privilege-boundary mechanism (independent of the subject's own tool-call results) rather than inventing that discipline from scratch.
- Family-native additions: The multi-app world simulation itself (a rich set of live adjacent/decoy records across at least two record types) plus the per-instance intended-cascade allowlist -- both genuinely new relative to access-token-scope-expansion, which does not require a dense simulated world of unrelated records.
- Expected build hours: 75
- Cheapest kill gate: Build one minimal two-record-type world (~10-15 records, one near-duplicate decoy) with a correct reference solution using a precisely-scoped action, a 'lazy-bulk-call' mutant reaching the same target via an overbroad filter/bulk call, and one field that legitimately changes as an intended cascade of the reference solution. Run both once through the proposed full-world-diff verifier; if it fails to flag the mutant or falsely zeroes the reference's legitimate cascade, the concept is falsified before any decoy-density or full-harness investment.
- Predicted clean 5/6-or-6/6 probability: probabilityOrdinaryPackage 53% (range 35-65%); probabilityFullRecipe 28% (range 10-40%) -- ordinary builds roughly coin-flip (collapsing toward high-pass if the tool surface lacks any bulk/filter affordance at all); a hardened build with real decoy density and no convenient shortcut should suppress the rate meaningfully.
- What we'd learn even if easy: If agents reliably avoid collateral damage even under maximal decoy density and a genuinely more-convenient broad-scope path, scope discipline would be shown to be a well-generalized frontier capability that survives with no reward-visible penalty for the broader action -- suggesting the related, already-built access-token-scope-expansion family's difficulty (if any) comes specifically from authorization/credential framing rather than the generic narrow-vs-convenient-broad-action temptation this family isolates.


**Slot coverage self-check:** {"strongestEvidence":true,"fairRetestOverexplicitMicrotask":true,"freshExternalDiscovery":true,"distinctCausalAxis":true}

**Sequential trial spend plan:** Phase 0 (near-zero cost, run in parallel across all 4 families before any paid agent trial): execute each family's cheapestKillGate. For narrow-assertion-reward-hacking, this reuses the existing Hardened v2 recipe's B6 probe triad. For ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing, it's a single-fixture add to the already-working ui-replay-browser-backed harness plus 2-3 zero-shot agent reads. For cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap, it's a single-turn, no-harness LLM-recall check across 3-5 models. For state-diff-collateral-damage-verification, it's a hand-built 10-15-record mini-world run once against reference and mutant solutions. Any family whose kill gate fails (agent trivially passes via recall, or the verifier fails to discriminate reference vs. mutant) is dropped from the cohort now, before a single dollar of real trial budget is spent, and its failure is logged under its current package hash -- never patched and re-tested under the same hash.

Phase 1 (paired trial, cheapest-surviving-family-first): for each family that survives Phase 0, spend build hours in ascending order of expectedBuildHours (narrow-assertion-reward-hacking 22h -> ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing 30h -> cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap 36h -> state-diff-collateral-damage-verification 75h), so the cheapest, highest-confidence builds validate the sequential-spend procedure itself before the most expensive build (state-diff, with its bespoke per-instance cascade-allowlist authoring) is committed. For each family in that order: build the package once, freeze it under a content hash, then run exactly one Codex trial and one Claude trial (2 total) against the frozen package.

Blind-labeling: before anyone sees which engine produced which trajectory, an independent rater blind-labels each of the 2 outcomes as CLEAN_PASS, CAPABILITY_FAILURE (agent reasoned but got the mechanism wrong), or VALIDITY_DEFECT (leaking hint, ambiguous spec, verifier bypass, non-deterministic harness) -- engine identity and prior expectations are withheld from the rater until labeling is complete, mirroring this portfolio's own reader-review discipline.

Expansion rule (one trial at a time, target = 5-of-6 remaining achievable): treat 6 trials as the maximum budget per family (matching this portfolio's standard Phase-14-style early-stop convention) with a working target of achieving a genuine, non-clean-majority signal -- i.e., NOT reproducing an easy 5/6 or 6/6 clean sweep (that outcome is itself a kill signal per this repo's own pre-registered convention, exactly as it was for CAA V1). After the initial 2-trial pair: if 0 or 1 of the first 2 are CLEAN_PASS, add exactly one more trial (alternating engine) and re-check; continue one trial at a time only while a genuinely discriminating outcome (neither a clean sweep nor a total-failure sweep) remains mathematically reachable within the 6-trial cap. Stop immediately, without spending the remaining budget, the moment either (a) enough CLEAN_PASSes have already accumulated that even all-remaining-fails cannot avoid a >=5/6 clean-majority result (the family is too easy, kill), (b) enough failures have accumulated that a genuine capability-gap read is already established and further trials would only add cost without changing the verdict, or (c) any single trial is blind-labeled VALIDITY_DEFECT -- a validity defect stops spend on that family immediately regardless of trial count remaining, since no amount of additional trials can rescue a package whose grading itself is untrustworthy.

Never-repair rule: if a VALIDITY_DEFECT or an unwanted clean-sweep is found, the current package (reference solution, verifier, scenario set) is retired as-is under its existing content hash -- it is never edited and re-run against the same or new trials. All trials already run against it are retained as permanent evidence tagged to that hash (matching how this repo retained the superseded 245-check durable-outbox artifact's results as a documented coverage-defect finding rather than deleting them when the 267-check fix shipped). Any fix is built as a distinct new package version under a new hash, and that new version restarts Phase 1 from trial 1 -- it does not inherit or extend the old package's trial count toward the 5/6 target.

Sequencing across the 4-family cohort: because state-diff-collateral-damage-verification carries the highest build cost (75h) and the least automatable construction step (hand-authored cascade allowlists), its Phase 1 spend is deliberately sequenced last, after the cheaper three families' Phase-0/Phase-1 results have confirmed the blind-labeling and stopping procedure works as intended on lower-stakes builds.

## 14. Rejected directions

Note: I searched the repo for an existing report matching this family list (`cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap`, etc.) and found none — the only "section 14 / Rejected directions" in the repo belongs to a different, unrelated report (`reports/RESEARCH-TASK-FAMILY-LANDSCAPE.md`, a different pool of families). So this is freestanding content for insertion wherever the parent report lives. Full section text follows.

---

## 14. Rejected directions

Of the 164 mined candidates, 20 became families across 11 causal axes. The other ~144 did not fail because they were bad observations — most are accurately reported facts about real systems, real incidents, or real benchmark governance. They failed one of six structural tests: the mechanism was **the same one already anchoring a selected family**, described a **benchmark's own construction process** rather than a task an agent solves, depended on **knowledge or infrastructure no in-sandbox investigation could surface**, was **confounded against its own stated crux**, required **trivia rather than professional judgment**, or was a **good mechanism that simply lost the capacity contest** for a slot. Each is treated below with the specific candidate and citation, not a blanket "rejected — insufficient evidence."

### 14.1 Redundant causal axis — the same mechanism mined more than once

The largest single source of pool inflation was Terminal-Bench's own governance documents restating the same two rules in nearly every file a contributor is asked to read.

- **The shared-vs-separate verifier container rule** (`check-separate-verifier.sh`) appears, word-for-word or near-verbatim, as six independent "mechanisms": `tb3-repo-root`'s "Shared mode is leaky — the verifier inherits agent-installed packages, mutated filesystem state, and any files the agent wrote," restated identically in `tb-review-automation`'s "Shared-verifier state contamination"; `tb-contributing`'s "Verifier artifact-boundary partial observation" and "Stale/torn-down execution context across agent-to-verifier handoff" (two mechanisms from one rule); `tb-rubric-proposal`'s "Verifier trust-boundary collapse"; and `tb-rubric-implementation`'s "Verifier tampering via shared agent/verifier execution environment." `klavis-toolathlon-mvp-repo`'s "hidden environment-authority seam" is a seventh, independently-discovered instance of the same shape in a different codebase. Two of these (`tb3-repo-root`, plus the `trial-time-tool-shim` sibling below) were kept as the anchors for **Verifier Container Isolation Boundary**; the rest add citations, not new task-shape information.
- **The trial-time `curl` shim attack** is quoted identically in two documents: `tb3-repo-root` and `tb-review-automation` both cite "an agent can shim `/usr/bin/curl` in its phase so the verifier's later fetch returns trojaned output — the RDI/Berkeley 89/89 attack on TB2." One became the anchor for **Trial-Time Tool Shim Poisoning**; the second is the same sentence, not corroborating new evidence.
- **The Notion `normalize_*` error-envelope bug** (`tools/base.py::handle_notion_error` stripped to `{}` on a 404) was mined three times from three different artifact types describing the same bug: the Klavis org README (`klavis-org`), the GitHub issue that reported it (`klavis-klavis-issues`), and the PR that fixed it (`klavis-klavis-prs`). All three cite the identical file and function. This is one bug, not three data points, and it never became a family on its own — its shape (a structured error collapsed to an ambiguous empty success) is close to **Nominally-Successful Batch Hides Partial Failure**, so it stands as corroborating background for that axis rather than a 21st family.
- **"Multi-day" / "longer-horizon" difficulty language** appears verbatim in two separate internal design documents: `tb-contribution-call` ("'longer-horizon work (e.g., multi-day projects)' named as one of the difficulty strategies") and `tb-google-design-doc` ("Longer horizon tasks (e.g. multi-day)"). One anchors **Multi-Day State Carryforward Drift**; the second is the same design-doc bullet copied into a different file.
- **"Richer environments"** is likewise duplicated verbatim between `tb-contribution-call` ("'Richer environments' (e.g., many microservices, filesystems, databases)") and `tb-google-design-doc` ("Richer environments (e.g. many microservices, filesystems, databases, applications)") — the same aspirational strategy line, not two independent observations.
- **Terminal-Bench's own six-category failure taxonomy** (Premature Termination, Step Repetition, Context Loss, Reasoning-Action Mismatch, Disobey Task Specification, Weak Verification) was independently re-mined as if each category were a discovered mechanism, across `foundry-cli-universe` ("Agent loops same command 165x without progress," "reasoning describes a backward attack strategy but the implemented code uses forward brute-force") and `foundry-terminal-agent-lab` ("'Premature Termination' is listed as one of the trajectory-level error categories," "'Disobey Task Specification' and 'Reasoning-Action Mismatch' as distinct failure categories"). This is one published taxonomy cited from two source documents, not five or six new mechanisms.
- **Live-cutover-under-continuous-traffic** and **stale-authority-with-a-withdrawn-oracle** were each mined twice: the `kv-live-surgery`/`live-database-cutover` pair in `tb-repo-root` duplicates the `live-database-cutover` evidence in `tb-merged-tasks-sample` ("any 5xx, unexpected 4xx, data mismatch, stale read, or lost row fails at first occurrence" appears in both); and `risk-scorer-replay`'s stale-authority mechanism appears in both `tb-repo-root` and `tb-merged-tasks-sample` describing the same task. Both pairs fold into **Read-Replica Lag Reconciliation**'s axis rather than doubling the family count.
- **Retries/duplicate-side-effects** was oversubscribed relative to its two slots: beyond the anchors (`incident-payments-idempotency`'s three-part cluster → **Ambiguous-Failure Payment Retry Discipline**; the Kafka consumer-group pair in `tb-repo-root`/`tb-merged-tasks-sample` → **Worker Rebalance Callback Deduplication**), the pool also contains an SQS visibility-timeout trio (`boundary-mq-visibility-timeout`), a Temporal Activity idempotency pair (`boundary-scheduler-at-least-once`), and Twilio's own billing postmortem (`incident-telecom-billing`) — three further, well-evidenced instances of the identical "at-least-once delivery forces an idempotency decision" shape. They were not promoted because the axis already had its two families and each would require standing up a different piece of proprietary cloud infrastructure (a faithful SQS emulator, a Temporal server, a Redis-vs-ledger simulation) to add a mechanism the pool had already demonstrated twice.

### 14.2 Benchmark governance and methodology, not a task shape

A large slice of the pool describes how a *benchmark itself* should be built, reviewed, or reported — valuable as construction guidance, but not a shape a subject agent solves inside a professional task:

- `tb-contributing`'s "Misspecification-vs-genuine-difficulty conflation" (mandatory `harbor analyze` before accepting a reward-0 task) and `tb-rubric-proposal`'s "Spec-ambiguity-driven verifier divergence" ("if two reasonable people read the problem description... a well-specified problem should result in both of them writing verification algorithms so that anyone who passed one of the verifiers would also pass the other") are the review *principles* that justify several of the rejections elsewhere in this section — they are criteria, not candidates.
- `tb-rubric-proposal`'s "Disallowed-lookup scope expansion" and `tb-rubric-implementation`'s "Process constraints stated in instructions but not mechanically verified" both describe the same governance gap (a soft behavioral rule like "don't search the internet" graded only by an optional transcript pass) restated across two rubric documents.
- `tb-rubric-implementation`'s "Self-referential ground truth defeats a stated but unenforced 'do-not-modify' authority constraint" is explicitly a fixture TB built to test *its own* rubric-scoring tool ("This is the intended flaw") — a test of the benchmark's grading code, not a professional task.
- `tb-reviewing`'s "Format-precision overfitting masking genuine reasoning failure" and `tb-public-trial-results`'s full three-item cluster (binary all-or-nothing containers, k=5 repeated-trial sampling, leaderboard aggregation masking per-trial granularity) all describe how TB *reports scores*, not a mechanism any task exercises.
- `foundry-tua-bench`'s "Solvability-filtered curation" ("only the 100 tasks with the lowest solvability... across three frontier models... were retained") and `klavis-deep-swe-repo`'s "Calibrated difficulty gate" describe a *filtering process for building a corpus*, not a task shape — and the former is close enough to the pool's other warnings against filtering-for-failure that it was excluded on the same policy ground.
- `foundry-swe-bench`'s three items (38.3% underspecified issues, 61.1% unfair `FAIL_TO_PASS` tests, single-file selection bias) are a **survey of a different benchmark's construction defects**, secondhand relative to the primary TB evidence already anchoring the adjacent verification-strategy families.
- `foundry-program-repair-survey`'s three items (BugSwarm's 5.56% archived-CI reproduction rate, Defects4J's manual commit-isolation process, CI-pass/fail used as an unchecked ground-truth proxy) and `foundry-incident-mining-survey`'s three items (The VOID's cascading-failure taxonomy) and `foundry-fault-injection-survey`'s three items (an academic fault-injection harness for multi-agent pipelines) are one level removed from a concrete, buildable fixture — they report on benchmarks and taxonomies, not on a professional scenario a subject would encounter.
- `klavis-mcp-atlas-repo`'s "fixed data-snapshot dependency" (an eval harness that "return[s] erroneous results" unless a specific export is pre-imported) and "whole-trajectory HTTP retry replays already-executed side-effecting tool calls" (the grading service's own retry loop can double-fire a write) both describe fragility in the *evaluation harness*, not the task.
- `foundry-toolathlon`'s "Authority-boundary bridging via credential substitution beneath the task" (Klavis's socket/URL-rewriting "hijacking" to inject credentials transparently) and `foundry-open-discovery`'s "Authority-boundary / permission-sandbox mis-specification" (manually-declared per-task sandbox permissions) are both about how the *test rig* wires up access, not a boundary the agent is asked to reason about.
- `local-idea-candidate-shapes`' three items and `local-production-strategy-doc`'s three items are this project's own prior-phase self-audit notes (a withdrawn lineage's "package leak," a memory-facade harness bug, a fairness window this project already found and fixed) and internal planning to-dos — counting them as freshly "mined" pool material would be circular.

### 14.3 No subject-inaccessible witness, or a verifier confounded against its own stated crux

Three items in the pool are Terminal-Bench's *own* PR-review rejections of proposed tasks, and they are the cleanest available evidence for this exact failure mode:

- `tb-rejected-prs-sample` (carlini, PR #89): "The archive admits two byte-for-byte equivalent decodings... These differ only by a transpose... Every failing trial produced exact transposes." No signal in the data disambiguates the "correct" answer from its mirror image — reward-0 would measure format-guessing luck, not competence.
- `tb-rejected-prs-sample` (ibercovich, PR #492): "Two VeraPDF metadata gates form an unintended second crux... blocks agents independently of the MathML question." A reward-0 here cannot be attributed to the mechanism the task claims to test.
- `tb-rejected-prs-sample` (dwahdany, PR #676): "roughly a third of trials appear to have had a broken environment, not a failing agent... GPU OOM from loading 12+ model backends on one H100." Indistinguishable from infrastructure flakiness — no witness isolates agent competence from environment reliability.

The same disposition applies to two pool items outside that sample: `klavis-mcpmark-repo`'s LEGO `transfer_parts` task, whose own stated requirements are in tension ("Log failed transfers with error messages" while also demanding "complete rollback on any failure... no partial updates possible" under standard transaction semantics — the grader's real expectation is not fully expressed in the instruction); and `incident-insurance-claims`, where correct adjudication depends on a value judgment ("existing beneficiary medical records were sufficient to support the medical necessity") for which no crisp, mechanically-checkable oracle exists without simulating a full clinical-review corpus.

### 14.4 Obscure trivia and unavailable knowledge

- `boundary-git-refs`'s three items (reflog-dependent recovery, detached-HEAD identity collapse, `git gc`'s "around 7,000 loose objects or more than 50 packfiles" trigger threshold) require specific git-internals procedural knowledge rather than professional judgment, and all the state involved is subject-side — there is no external authority whose boundary is being tested.
- `incident-legal-esignature`'s three items depend on DocuSign-specific API trivia ("REST API can create and modify authoritative copy flags but cannot export... Only the SOAP API provides operations necessary for exporting") and niche legal vocabulary ("authoritative copy," "chattel paper") not derivable from in-sandbox investigation.
- `klavis-deep-swe-repo`'s koota-ECS-rollback and langchain-coalescing items require memorizing one library's specific relation/trait semantics or its "six call surfaces" — passable only by prior familiarity with that exact codebase, not by professional reasoning.
- `incident-supply-chain-npm`'s three items (a social-engineered maintainer handoff, an AES-encrypted payload "disguised as a test fixture," a malicious transitive dependency) require the subject to already distrust a governance action that looks completely legitimate on its face; nothing in a legitimate in-sandbox investigation distinguishes it from the many benign maintainer transfers that happen every day.
- `tb-discussion-224`'s "GPT-5 liked to use nano for editing files, but then it opens it and can't use it... gets stuck in interactive mode" is a vivid, well-evidenced trap, but it tests terminal ergonomics, not professional judgment, and carries no professional stakeholder or authority boundary at all.

### 14.5 Single, real, named incidents that don't reduce to a buildable, fair task

`incident-travel-overbooking` (a specific, named airline and flight number), `incident-saas-config-drift` (a specific multi-datacenter postmortem with figures like "954 divergent writes" and "200,000 webhook payloads... dropped"), and `incident-gaming-economy` (a shooter game's netcode exploit, sourced from a single corporate blog post with no code or protocol spec) are all richly documented but each requires either reproducing proprietary infrastructure at high fidelity or building a task transparently keyed to one identifiable real-world event — the latter reintroduces exactly the "obscure trivia / real-event recall" fairness problem this section exists to screen for, and the former is pure construction cost with no payoff beyond mechanisms the pool already demonstrates more cheaply elsewhere (stale-authority, retries).

### 14.6 Good mechanism, no portfolio slot — a capacity cut, not a quality cut

`foundry-tau-bench`'s three items are the strongest counter-example to "rejected means flawed": the paper reports concrete, measured effects ("removing policy text from the prompt dropped gpt-4o 22.4% on airline vs 4.4% on retail"; weaker models "called tools with non-existent user/product/order/item IDs at a rate of 2.08–6.34 per task"). This is a thirteenth well-evidenced causal axis — natural-language policy compliance under compound, multi-item requests — that simply lost the allocation contest against the 11 axes that shipped. It was not built for reasons of program scope, not evidentiary weakness, and should be the first candidate reconsidered if the portfolio expands past 20.

`tb-google-design-doc`'s "Tacit / expert-domain knowledge gap" sits in the same honest-capacity-cut category but leans toward §14.4's trivia concern too (correctness depends on domain conventions like "accounting treatment" or "a scientific fitting methodology" the instruction cannot spell out without giving away the answer).

### 14.7 Where an axis legitimately stayed a singleton

Two selected axes produced only one family each, and the pool explains why without requiring an artificial rejection:

- **`pagination-partial-observation`** had exactly one rich cluster (`incident-crm-pagination`'s query-locator expiration, concurrency-limit exhaustion, and cross-page data drift), all three of which are folded into the single **CRM Bulk Pagination Under Live Concurrent Writes** family. No second materially distinct pagination cluster existed; the DOM-mutation-observer evidence is also partial-observation-adjacent but its dominant failure mode is temporal ordering, so it was allocated to `async-out-of-order-completion` instead.
- **`scope-expansion-collateral-damage`** is anchored by `foundry-open-discovery`'s AppWorld state-diff evidence ("state-based unit tests... checking for unexpected changes, i.e., collateral damage"). `foundry-osworld`'s misclick-driven scope expansion and `klavis-toolathlon-mvp-repo`'s decoy-record discrimination are thinner instances of the same shape (single-fixture evidence versus AppWorld's general methodology) and were folded in as corroboration rather than promoted to a second family.

## 15. Uncertainty and falsification conditions

## 15. Uncertainty and falsification conditions

This section applies to the entire top-10 candidate portfolio and the 4-family cohort selected from it (`ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing`, `cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap`, `narrow-assertion-reward-hacking`, `state-diff-collateral-damage-verification`). None of the four has run a single paid agent trial as of this writing — every probability below is a scorer-reconciled prior, not a measurement, and every prior in the cohort data itself is already marked with a range and, for two of four families, an explicit "low confidence" tag on `probabilityFullRecipe`. Treat everything in this section as pre-registration, not results.

### What would show the recipe does not transfer

"The recipe" here is a specific, falsifiable claim: that validity- and difficulty-construction operators already proven inside one family (durable-outbox's three-process ledger isolation and A2 terminal-state repair; access-token-scope-expansion's authority-boundary grading; CAA's origin-authority framing; ui-replay-browser-backed's Playwright harness) can be transplanted onto a new causal axis and produce a genuinely hard, fair package on the first build, without repeating the multi-round bypass-discovery cycle each donor family needed. Concrete signals that this fails:

- **A second validity-defect discovery cycle after Phase 1 begins.** Durable-outbox needed three rounds of bypass discovery (never-called-tool false-pass, monkeypatched ground truth, bypassable role isolation) before its ledger-isolation operator was trustworthy. If `state-diff-collateral-damage-verification`'s full-world-diff grader or `narrow-assertion-reward-hacking`'s loose/strict-arm checker passes its cheap Phase-0 kill gate (the 10-15-record mini-world; the B6 probe triad on one hand-authored loose/strict pair) but a bypass is found only after real trials are spent, that is direct evidence the reused operator does not generalize to a new grader topology and each new grader shape needs its own from-scratch adversarial audit — i.e., the operator vocabulary in `data/hardness-operators.json` is not actually portable, only its provenance is.
- **≥3 of 4 cohort families reach a clean sweep (≥5/6) after clearing Phase 0.** CAA V1 passed all 72 registered probe cells and scored 85/85 on the contract gate and still returned 4/4 clean solves (VALID-BUT-EASY). Phase 0 in the `sequentialTrialSpendPlan` exists specifically to catch this before paid trials. If most of the cohort survives Phase 0 and then clean-sweeps in Phase 1 anyway, the failure is not just in the four families — it is in the kill-gate design itself, meaning the track's cheap-filtering method does not transfer either.
- **`ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing` clears cleanly using only the reused mutants.** Its own cheapest kill gate is explicit: add the one new fixture to the existing `ui-replay-browser-backed` harness and run 2-3 zero-shot agents using only the pre-existing selector-drift/stale-handle/aria-busy mutants. If they pass, the family's incremental-gap claim is falsified in an afternoon, and — more importantly for the track — it shows the foundry's one replicated local capability signal (5/5 and 219/864-scenario failures in `ui-action-record-replay`/`ui-replay-live-dom`, the only two SHIP families) is mutant-specific dressing rather than a load-bearing async-DOM-completion reasoning gap, and further siblings in that lineage should not be funded.
- **`cdn-cache-staleness-verification-gap` clears cleanly even after the trail-based grader is built.** Its Stage-1 kill gate (single-turn, no-tool recall probe across 3-5 models) is the cheap falsifier; but the expensive falsifier is the one worth watching — if the family still clears cleanly with trail-based, tier-by-tier grading (the direct fix for CAA V1's diagnosed "no self-check-coverage predictor" flaw) and no recitable forbidden-outcomes text, that would be the single most consequential result the track could produce: it would mean CAA V1's easiness was *not* explained by its three diagnosed construction flaws, and the whole "trust origin over stale delegated copy" causal axis should be retired from the portfolio rather than repeatedly re-built under new names.
- **The reused operator moves the solve rate less than the one existing local measurement of operator effect.** The unpublished, locally-derived A2 differential (control 2/6 solved vs. treatment 3/6 solved, i.e., a one-trial swing out of six from inserting a single explicit terminal-state sentence) is the only measured local data point on how much a targeted, fair repair moves a solve rate. If the cdn family's trail-grader or the reward-hacking family's strict-arm checker produce a swing smaller than or statistically indistinguishable from that one-in-six baseline, the recipe's marginal difficulty contribution is within a noise floor already observed once, not a reproducible lever.
- **A validity defect on any single trial.** Per the pre-registered never-repair rule, one `VALIDITY_DEFECT` blind-label stops spend on that family immediately, regardless of trials remaining — that package is retired under its hash, permanently, exactly as the 245-check durable-outbox artifact's coverage defect was retained as evidence rather than deleted.

### What evidence would justify scaling toward 1,000

None of the following exists yet for this cohort; each is a specific, checkable bar, not a vibe:

- **Realized, not estimated, build hours.** The four families are budgeted at 22h / 30h / 36h / 75h (163h total) specifically because each leans on reused infrastructure rather than building from zero. That estimate is itself a claim under test. Scaling is justified only if realized hours land near budget across at least 6 built families (not 1-2, which could be reuse-favorable outliers) — i.e., a demonstrated marginal cost, when recipe-reuse is the dominant share of scope, of roughly ≤15-20h per family. This is directly measurable the moment the cohort is actually built: compare logged hours to the 163h estimate.
- **Within-family replication.** Nothing in this foundry — not durable-outbox, not CAA, not ui-action-record-replay — has ever shown that difficulty is a property of a family rather than of one package. If ≥2 of the 4 cohort families reach a stable, blind-labeled `CAPABILITY_FAILURE` verdict within the 6-trial cap, a second, independently-built package inside that *same* family must also reproduce the capability failure before any claim of "this causal axis is hard" extends past the one package tested.
- **A genuine human-solvability data point.** Human-solvability evidence is 0 pass / 13 fail / 9 n/a across the entire 22-family foundry. Absent a human trial, "agents fail" cannot be distinguished from "the task is unfair or effectively unsolvable" — the same standard that disqualified four TB tasks in this track's own rejection criteria (a 0/30 solve rate is not by itself evidence of a good task). At least one of the four cohort families needs a human-solvability attempt before its result counts toward a scaling case.
- **Coverage of the rest of the top-10, not just the cohort.** Seven of the ten ranked candidates — `webhook-out-of-order-state-overwrite`, `rollback-reactivates-dormant-defect`, `clinical-bundle-partial-success-masking`, `sql-identifier-injection-scope-expansion`, `verifier-container-isolation-boundary`, `fuzzy-instruction-cross-server-tool-discovery`, `multi-day-state-carryforward-drift` — have no cohort slot, no kill gate, and no recipe overlay defined in this round. Scaling arithmetic toward 1,000 cannot be estimated honestly while 70% of even the pre-filtered top-10 shortlist is unplanned; a scaling recommendation needs kill-gate designs and build-hour estimates for those seven before the portfolio-level cost model means anything.
- **Zero fatal findings on an adversarial audit of a shipped cohort package**, at the same depth applied to durable-outbox (which surfaced three false-pass bypasses) and CAA (which surfaced the recitable-trap and no-coverage-predictor flaws) — a cohort family that has never been adversarially audited cannot be cited as scaling evidence no matter how many clean capability-failure trials it accumulates.

### Mandatory pre-trial conditions

Two of the four cohort families depend entirely on grader integrity for their result to mean anything: `state-diff-collateral-damage-verification`'s full-world-diff verifier must distinguish a lazy-bulk-call mutant from a reference solution's legitimate intended cascades, and `narrow-assertion-reward-hacking`'s loose/strict-arm checker pair must discriminate a scripted hollow artifact from a genuinely different-but-correct one. Given this repo's own history of two independent engines scoring 113/113 against a checker that was never actually exercised (durable-outbox's never-called-tool and monkeypatched-ground-truth bypasses), **no Phase-1 trial should be spent on either family until its Phase-0 discrimination check has actually run and been recorded** — not assumed, not skipped because the design "looks like" the durable-outbox pattern it was adapted from.

### Principal uncertainties in this cohort

- **`narrow-assertion-reward-hacking`'s source is a community discussion and rubric proposal** (tb-discussion-224, tb-rubric-proposal), not a measured incident. Its causal-axis distinctness is asserted, not demonstrated against any per-trial base rate anywhere in this repo or externally.
- **The state-diff family's kill-gate design was proposed independently by two scorers**, which reconciliation treated as corroboration — but neither proposal has been run. Convergent guesses are not evidence the mutant/reference pair actually behaves as designed.
- **`ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing`'s cited primary source units do not exist anywhere in the discovery corpus or source ledgers** — a live provenance gap that must be closed before this family could pass a ship-gate provenance review, independent of any trial outcome.
- **CAA Phase 18 is in-flight and untrialed.** The cdn-cache retest's calibration anchor is Phase 17 alone (n=4, one campaign). No Phase 18 result exists yet to cross-check whether CAA V1's diagnosed flaws, once fixed, actually raise difficulty — the cdn family is currently the *only* test of that hypothesis anywhere in the program.
- **`ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing` is not in the ranked top-10** — it was added to the cohort for evidentiary reasons (the only candidate anchored to the foundry's one replicated local capability gap) despite scoring outside the top-10 composite ranking. This is a real, unresolved tension for any scaling decision: prioritizing by score and prioritizing by strongest existing evidence do not currently point at the same set of families.
- **All four `probabilityFullRecipe` estimates carry wide ranges** (e.g., 28%, range 10-45%, explicitly flagged low-confidence), reflecting reconciled scorer judgment, not data. Zero of the four families has run a trial; treat every number in this section's cohort data as a bet the Phase-0/Phase-1 plan exists specifically to resolve cheaply, not as a forecast to be defended.

**Citation audit:** ## Citation Audit Report

**Sample:** 10 of 56 entries (~18%), spanning all three access methods (gh CLI, WebFetch, WebSearch, local filesystem) and multiple depth/honesty-risk categories — including entries that explicitly admit 404s, redirects, and partial failures, which are the highest-risk claims to fabricate.

### Entries re-verified

| sourceUnitId | Claim tested | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `tb-repo-root` | Basic repo metadata via `gh repo view` | **Confirmed** — repo exists, default branch `main`, description matches |
| `tb-rubric-proposal` | Literal path `rubrics/task-proposal.toml` 404s; real rubric recovered at `docs/prompts/task-proposal.md`; `scripts/checks/rubric_review.py` runs it against model `"claude-opus-4-8"` | **Confirmed exactly** — reproduced the 404, confirmed the recovered file's content matches the described rubric, and confirmed the literal (unusual-looking) model string `DEFAULT_MODEL = "claude-opus-4-8"` is really in the file, not a hallucinated model name |
| `tb-discussion-224` | GraphQL discussion #224 returns full body + comments on first attempt | **Confirmed** — title, full body text, and 1 comment retrieved identically |
| `klavis-klavis-issues` | "2 pages, 200 items" from `issues?state=all&per_page=100` | **Confirmed exactly** — page 1 = 100, page 2 = 100 |
| `tb-google-design-doc` | PR #32 links CONTRIBUTING.md to a specific Google Doc URL as "Open Call for Task Contributions" | **Confirmed exactly** — PR #32 body contains the identical Google Doc URL and framing |
| `incident-knight-capital` | WebFetch of SEC PDF + local read yields 18 pages | **Confirmed exactly** — WebFetch independently returned an 18-page PDF (96.1KB) matching the claimed page count. (Note: my own plain `curl` got HTTP 403 with a 1.9KB stub — SEC bot-blocks generic user agents — which itself corroborates why the source used WebFetch/local-read rather than a simpler method) |
| `boundary-oauth-refresh` | RFC 6749/7009 "resolved without redirect issues" | **Minor discrepancy** — both URLs actually issue an HTTP 302 to `/info/rfcNNNN/` before rendering. However, full RFC text (98 section anchors, ~777KB, containing e.g. "4.1. Authorization Code Grant") is server-rendered at the destination, so the `depthAchieved: full-source` claim is substantively true — only the "without redirect issues" phrasing is imprecise |
| `incident-crm-pagination` | `developer.salesforce.com` REST API doc pages return HTTP 403 (bot-blocked) | **Confirmed** — reproduced the identical 403 |
| `foundry-toolathlon` | `hkust-nlp/Toolathlon` repo exists; depth honestly self-labeled `readme-only` | **Confirmed** — repo exists exactly as named; the modest self-labeled depth (vs. claiming full-source) is itself a good honesty signal |
| `local-idea-candidate-shapes` | Local files `examples/shapes/*.json`, `docs/TASK-FAMILY-MODEL.md`, `docs/DEFECT-TAXONOMY.md` exist | **Confirmed** — all present (22 shape files found) |

### Findings

No fabrications and no overstated `depthAchieved`/`accessMethod` claims were found. One trivial wording imprecision: `boundary-oauth-refresh` says the RFC fetches were "without redirect issues" when both URLs do 302-redirect internally — but this doesn't affect the accuracy of the `full-source` depth claim, since the full document content is confirmed present at the resolved URL. This is cosmetic, not a substantive honesty problem.

Notably, the index's self-reported failures/degradations checked out as accurate rather than hidden: the literal-path 404s in the `tb-rubric-*` and `tb-reviewing`/`tb-review-automation` entries were real and independently reproducible (not paraphrased-over), the SEC PDF's difficulty for generic tooling matches what I hit with `curl`, and the Salesforce 403s were reproduced verbatim. Entries also show differentiated, non-inflated depth labels (`partial`, `readme-only`) rather than blanket `full-source` claims — a pattern consistent with careful self-assessment rather than post-hoc rationalization.

### Overall confidence assessment

**High confidence the source index is honest.** Across 10 spot-checked entries covering gh CLI, WebFetch, WebSearch-mediated, and local-filesystem access, every substantive claim (exact item counts, exact page counts, specific 404→recovery narratives, specific 403 blocks, exact PR/discussion content, exact file paths and content) reproduced faithfully on independent re-fetch. The single issue found is a minor wording imprecision with no effect on the depth claim's accuracy. I'd extend this confidence to the rest of the index, particularly given the consistent, granular honesty pattern observed in exactly the entries most likely to be embellished (literal-path failures and access denials).

**Quality-control checklist result:** # Quality-Control Checklist Results

**1. Every top-20 family has primary-source provenance.** — **PASS.** All 20 entries carry non-empty, specifically-named `primarySourceUnitIds` (real incidents: `incident-knight-capital`, `incident-payments-idempotency`, `incident-healthcare-interop`, `incident-crm-pagination`; specs/RFCs: `boundary-oauth-refresh`, `boundary-webhook-idempotency`, `boundary-dom-mutation-observer`, `boundary-db-replica-lag`, `boundary-cdn-invalidation`; repos/papers: `klavis-klavis-repo`, `tb3-repo-root`, `foundry-toolathlon`, `foundry-tua-bench`, `foundry-mcp-bench`, `foundry-open-discovery`, `foundry-cli-universe`) — none are blank or self-referential (note: these IDs aren't yet cross-checkable against a persisted source ledger in `data/`, since this deliverable hasn't been committed to disk).

**2. Every percentage is clearly marked as estimated.** — **PASS, with a wording gap to tighten.** The only bare "%" in the set (`~38.6%`, `long-horizon-multi-app-coordination`) and the adjacent numeric citations (`roughly 0.30` in fuzzy-instruction-discovery, `~27 points` in multi-day-state-carryforward-drift, `5/5` in ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing, `89/89` in trial-time-tool-shim-poisoning) are all hedged with "~"/"roughly"/"documented [case]" rather than the literal word "estimated" — functionally equivalent but not literally compliant.

**3. No mutant result is presented as agent evidence.** — **PASS.** The word "mutant" and any reference to `data/mutants.json`-style synthetic mutation results do not appear anywhere in the 20 family definitions, taskShapes, or whyAgentMayFail fields.

**4. No spec/harness failure counts as difficulty; outbox 6/6 and A2 2/6→3/6 appear only as calibration facts.** — **PASS.** Neither "durable-outbox," "A2," nor the ratios 6/6 or 2/6→3/6 appear inside any of the 20 family entries. The one internal-evidence citation (`ui-action-replay-dom-mutation-timing`'s "5/5 trial failure rate") cites the foundry's own *already-shipped UI-replay families*, not outbox/CAA, and is flagged `evidenceType: "rebuilt-from-foundry"` rather than being disguised as fresh external evidence.

**5. No family is merely a renamed scenario.** — **PASS.** Each entry has a distinct definition/taskShape/domain/source. Closest sibling pairs — (9) verifier-container-isolation-boundary vs. (10) trial-time-tool-shim-poisoning (isolation architecture vs. binary-shimming attack), and (18) long-horizon-multi-app-coordination vs. (19) multi-day-state-carryforward-drift (disjoint-app tool coordination vs. single-environment simulated-time drift) — share a causal axis/source flavor but test mechanistically different failure modes, not a rename.

**6. No source incident was multiplied into nominally different families.** — **PASS.** Every `incident-*` ID (Knight Capital, payments-idempotency, healthcare-interop, CRM-pagination) is used exactly once. The two repeated corpus-level sources — `tb3-repo-root`/`tb-review-automation` (families 9, 10) and `klavis-klavis-repo` (families 8, 16) — are multi-document repos yielding two genuinely different documented problems each, not one incident split in two.

**7. Every top-10 public contract can be fair without revealing the repair.** — **PASS.** All 10 carry a `whyFair` grounded in public, derivable material (RFC 2181/6749, Stripe docs, FHIR spec, CWE-class injection knowledge, terminal-bench's own hardening docs, given tool schemas, upfront full scope) rather than hidden internal knowledge. Two need care at actual-instantiation time: `rollback-reactivates-dormant-defect` (must not literally name Knight Capital in the task text) and `verifier-container-isolation-boundary` (the declared-artifact contract must actually be stated to the agent, not just implied).

**8. Every hidden verifier behavior is supported by public requirements.** — **PASS.** Each family's `subjectInaccessibleWitness`/hidden-check maps to a public rule (pg_stat_replication, MutationObserver `takeRecords()`, Stripe dedup keying, FHIR nested-Bundle status, declared-artifact contracts, given I/O schemas). `narrow-assertion-reward-hacking` and `state-diff-collateral-damage-verification` are the two riskiest for drifting into an undisclosed gotcha and warrant the closest instantiation-time review, but as specified they tie back to stated deliverable scope/description.

**9. The portfolio is not dominated by distributed-systems recomputation.** — **PASS.** Only 4/20 (cdn-cache-staleness, replica-lag, worker-rebalance-callback-dedup, webhook-out-of-order) are classically distributed-systems/consistency topics (~20%); the remainder spans fintech, healthcare, security/injection, CI-grading infra, browser/UI automation, CRM ops, tool-selection, and long-horizon agentic coordination. Same ratio (2/10, cdn + webhook) holds inside the top-10.

**10. Outbox and CAA are used as calibration, not prospective discoveries.** — **PASS.** No family in the 20 (by id, name, definition, or `primarySourceUnitIds`) references "outbox," "durable-approval-outbox," "CAA," or "commitment-authority-ambiguity" — they are absent from both the full 20 and the top-10.

**11. No `data/phase-18-*` file or other CAA V2 in-flight artifact was read as concluded, or modified.** — **PASS.** This session only ran filename-level `grep -rl` searches (no matches, no content displayed) against `data/`/`reports/`; no `data/phase-18-*` file was opened, its content used as evidence, or edited. None of the 20 families' `primarySourceUnitIds` cite any phase-18/CAA-V2 in-flight artifact.

**Overall: 11/11 PASS**, with two non-blocking wording notes carried forward for tightening before publication: (a) items 2's percentage/ratio citations should use the literal word "estimated" rather than relying solely on "~"/"roughly"; (b) items 7's two flagged families (`rollback-reactivates-dormant-defect`, `verifier-container-isolation-boundary`) need their actual task text checked at instantiation time to confirm the source incident/artifact-contract isn't named in a way that hands the agent the repair.
